It now runs once, as `cond-norm`, beside the pair norm that was already hoisted
for the same reason. (The pair norm holds for a whole schedule; this one moves
with the noise level, so it is per call.)

| | before | after |
|---|---:|---:|
| `adaln` | 1.42 ms | **1.36** |
| `ffw-adaln` | 1.39 | **1.34** |
| `cond-norm` | - | 0.020 |

**0.11 ms**, which is small - and is exactly the point. It is the measurement
that says the four reductions were never this kernel's problem, and it is what
made the K split's real cause findable.

## The sample dimension, as a second path

Built at the transformer, off by default, and the design constraint was that
**the one-sample path must not move at all**. It does not:
`tools/gpu/check-difftx-samples.js` generates every shader at `samples: 1`
against a build with the parameter absent and requires them identical character
for character, across four arms - plain, split-K, norm-split, everything at
once - and fifteen shaders. A fold gate would catch a wrong kernel; it would
not catch a slower one, and this stack's history is of changes that were
correct and cost 20%.

Above one sample the row axis becomes `(sample, token)` and three things have to
know:

- **the conditioning is per TOKEN**, one tensor for every sample - which is the
  batching win, since a weight read and a conditioning read now serve S rows;
- **the pair bias likewise**, so a query row reduces to `i % TOKENS` before
  indexing it;
- 🔴 **and attention must not cross a sample.** Every other kernel batches for
  free on a wider row axis. `attend` does not: left alone it would attend across
  samples, which is not a slower answer but a different model. Its keys are
  bounded to `(i / TOKENS) * TOKENS`.

`tools/gpu/check-difftx-batched.js` is the test that catches all three, and the
only honest one: given S copies of one activation, every sample must come back
identical to the others AND to what one sample alone produces. Both failures
above are invisible at S=1 because at one sample they are the same expression.
**relRMS 0 at S = 1, 2, 4 and 8**, against one sample and between samples.

What it is worth, at 68 tokens, medians of five:

| samples | unbatched | batched | |
|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 2 | 24.20 ms | 16.10 | **1.50x** |
| 4 | 47.20 | 26.80 | **1.76x** |
| 8 | 92.80 | 45.70 | **2.03x** |

🔴 **AND THE HEAD CANNOT DRIVE IT YET.** The atom encoder, the atom decoder and
the sampler's positions and noise all still assume one sample, so nothing user
facing can ask for S > 1. What is done is the 61% of a step that is the
transformer, proven correct and measured; what is left is the plumbing around
it.

🔴 **AND A LATENT BUG FELL OUT OF DOING IT.** The pipeline cache key never
included `outKSplits`, `attnKSplits` or `normKSplits` - three edits that were
supposed to add them matched nothing and failed silently. Harmless for every
sweep in this file, which took each arm in its own process; a collision waiting
for two configurations in one. Fixed, with the sample count beside them.

## What to do, in the order the numbers rank it

A whole AF3 fold, 60 tokens and 50 diffusion steps, as the session went:

| | trunk | diffusion | total |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| f32 path (what an NVIDIA visitor gets) | 2.2 s | 4.4 s | **6.6 s** |
| plus the f16 toggle | 1.1 | 2.7 | 3.8 |
| plus the diffusion token tile | 1.2 | **2.2** | **3.4 s** |

**1.94x**, with mean pLDDT identical at every step.

1. ~~The f16 toggle~~ - done, and the largest single item: 1.74x on a fold and
   40% less device memory.
2. ~~The diffusion transformer's token tile~~ - done, 1.50x on the transformer
   at 59 tokens and 1.15x on a whole fold.
3. **Batch the sampler's SAMPLES** - and it says "seeds" no longer, because the
   two are different knobs and the difference is the whole point. A model seed
   reruns everything, trunk included; `num_diffusion_samples` reruns only the
   diffusion, which is what AF3 vmaps and what its own default sets to five.
   🔴 **AND THE 5x THIS ITEM CLAIMED IS STALE.** It rested on "the dense passes
   are ~28 ms a step whatever the size", which was true while they were
   underfilled and is not any more: they now run 10.65 ms at 68 tokens and
   21.19 at 272, four times the rows for twice the time. Measured on the built
   path, below: **1.5x at two samples, 1.76x at four, 2.03x at eight.** The
   slack batching was going to exploit has been spent by the splits and the
   occupancy work. Built for the transformer; the head cannot drive it yet.
4. ~~Chain the diffusion head on the device~~ - **already done**, and
   docs/PERF.md is stale in saying otherwise. The GPU is idle 4% of a step.
5. **Fix `probe-alu.js`** - the dead vector lanes and the degenerate f16
   constants, on both devices, and raise its default iteration count.
6. ~~A driver with `VK_KHR_cooperative_matrix`~~ - **done**, 535.129.03 ->
   570.195.03, and the units measure **310.9 TFLOP/s**, 8.6x the f16 vector
   path.
7. ~~Wire the matrix units into a kernel~~ - **done for AF2's transition**,
   which is 1.19x on a block and 3.2x less error. The staged kernel in
   `src/runtime/matrix-linear.js` is general; what is left is finding the next
   caller that materialises its operands.
8. **The remaining dense projections, in the order they cost.** AF2's
   `msa-*-attention.project` is 1.96 ms of an 8.77 ms block and `.output`
   another 0.91, and both are already GEMMs against a materialised source - but
   the projection writes FOUR differently-shaped, differently-typed outputs
   from one pass, so it needs the head reshape in the epilogue rather than a
   straight swap.
9. **AF3's transitions are the wrong target and this is worth writing down.**
   `single-transition` and `pair-transition` are 22% of a trunk pass between
   them, and they are FUSED: normalise, widen, swish-gate and contract in one
   dispatch, with the widened tensor never reaching global memory. Splitting
   that into two GEMMs would add two large intermediates and a pass, which is
   the trade the fusion already declined. AF2's transition was a good target
   BECAUSE it already materialises its hidden activation.
10. **AF3 at 59 tokens is not arithmetic-bound anywhere.** `single.project` is
   the single largest pass in a trunk at 14.7%, and it is 0.315 ms to project
   59 rows - occupancy and dispatch, not flops. The matrix units cannot help a
   shape that small; see the section above on the problem being too small.

### What AF2's matrix flash attention opens up for the other three models

Written after `src/evoformer/attention-matrix.js` reached **1.69x** on AF2's
four attentions and 18.06 -> 15.74 s on an 825-residue fold. Ranked, with the
evidence for each and what would falsify it.

11. **BUILT - `grid.attend` on the matrix units, 1.40x to 1.53x.** See "The
   pairformer's cubic kernel on the matrix units" below for the measurement and
   for the one AF2 finding that did NOT transfer. What follows is the case that
   was made for it, kept because it is what the next such call looks like.

   **`grid.attend` on the matrix units, for AF3 AND OpenDDE. The top item.**
   It is the same kernel shape AF2's was - a chunked flash attention with a
   staged key tile, whose own docstring names its cost as "the
   `2 * dimension/4` vectors a key needs", which is exactly the per-lane key
   read the units remove. Head dim is **32 for AF3**, identical to the AF2 case
   that went 1.69x, and **8 for OpenDDE**, which `attention-matrix.js` already
   supports and has a differential number for (1.20e-3). One kernel, two
   models, because OpenDDE runs AF3's pairformer.

   🔴 **AND IT IS THE ONE THAT GROWS FASTEST.** `grid.attend` is CUBIC in N -
   21.7 ms at 59 tokens against **318 at 150** - and this file already says "the
   exponent has not changed and it will lead again on a longer chain". Every
   other pairformer kernel is quadratic.

   🔴 **THE REASON IT WAS NEVER TRIED IS THE RULE AF2 JUST DISPROVED.** This
   file's own "materialised, past a billion MACs, K deep" was applied to AF2's
   flash attention and was WRONG: a GEMM at K = head_dim amortises its panel
   over two k steps, a flash attention stages the query tile once and reuses it
   across the whole key sequence. **That rule governs GEMMs and says nothing
   about an attention.** See docs/AF2.md.

   NOT a drop-in: different bindings, gating and pair-bias semantics, and the
   geometry MUST be re-swept - AF2's optimum moved from 6x16 to 4x32 when the
   scalar work in the body changed, with each 5-6% worse at the other's tile.

12. **BUILT - all four fold tools gate on the geometry now**, against one band
   in `tools/gpu/chain-geometry.js` rather than three copies of it, with
   `test/chain-geometry.test.js` checking the rule. `backboneGeometry` returns
   `worstCaca` beside the median, because the collapse AF2 hid was a median
   failure and a single link thrown across the box is not.

   **All four verified on device, each one both ways.** The bundles for the
   last two were not on this box at all, which is why the first version of this
   shipped with only the CPU test behind them; they are now.

       fold.js           median 3.824   worst 3.89    pLDDT 85.8
       fold-esmfold2.js         3.805         3.847
       fold-opendde.js          3.671         3.540         92.05
       fold-af2.js       already gated - see docs/AF2.md

   and each fires when the band is narrowed, with `--allow-broken-geometry`
   still reporting.

   🔴 **AND ONE OF THEM FIRED WITHOUT BEING ASKED TO, ON A REAL BROKEN FOLD.**
   The OpenDDE bundle rebuilt for this was quantised at **int5 group 128**,
   copying the `--bits 3 --group 128` that is ESM-C's setting; AF3's and
   OpenDDE's shipped bundles are both **group 32**. At group 128 OpenDDE folds
   6MRR to consecutive CA of **median 207.5 A, worst 3283.3 A** - a structure
   thrown across a third of a micron - and reports **pLDDT 46.69** for it. The
   same weights at group 32 give 3.671 A and pLDDT 92.05, which is docs/
   OPENDDE.md's recorded 92.051. So the failure this gate exists for is not
   hypothetical and is not only AF2's: it happened here, on the first fold
   after the gate went in, and the gate is what said so.

   **The geometry gate the other three fold tools do not have.** `fold.js`,
   `fold-esmfold2.js` and `fold-opendde.js` all COMPUTE the backbone geometry
   and none of them asserts on it. AF3's prints `N-CA`, `CA-C`, `CA-CA` and the
   radius of gyration under a comment saying *"GEOMETRY IS THE CHECK THAT
   MATTERS HERE, not pLDDT"*; OpenDDE's docstring opens *"the geometry is the
   gate before the fold is"*. Neither gates.

   That is exactly how AF2's 825-residue fold spent a whole optimisation
   campaign as a **collapsed ball at pLDDT 69.31** - consecutive CA 0.06 A
   apart - with every differential gate passing. `fold-af2.js` asserts now and
   the bands it uses are in docs/AF2.md. Cheap, and it guards every item above
   and below it. **A number a tool prints is not a gate until something fails
   on it.**

13. **BUILT - 1.51x on an ESMFold2 trunk's GPU time, 1.32x on its wall.** The
   split is wired through `compilePairTrack` behind `pairTransitionSplit`, and
   it is declined below `TRANSITION_SPLIT_MIN_CHANNELS` because at AF3's 128 it
   is 1.8% of a trunk for 72 MiB. See the section below.

   **UNBLOCKED - item 9's question is answered and the answer inverts by
   model.** See "Item 9 was right for AF3 and wrong for ESMFold2" below: at
   ESMFold2's 256 channels the fused kernel loses to two PLAIN GEMMs by 2.05x
   before the matrix units are involved at all, because what it holds in
   workgroup memory is the widened row and that doubles with the channel count.
   Not built. What follows is the case for it.

   **ESMFold2's `pair-transition`, but only after answering item 9.** It is
   **47-50% of the ESMFold2 trunk**, and that trunk is 85% of a 300-token
   fold's seconds - a far bigger share than the 22% item 9 weighed for AF3. Its
   `PAIR_CHANNELS` is **256**, the exact depth where AF2's transition measured
   28.2 TFLOP/s, against AF3's 128 where the staged path measures 14.9. And
   docs/EF2FAST.md establishes with three instruments that this kernel is
   **arithmetic-bound, not weight-read bound** - "every weight read that could
   be removed was already overlapped with the arithmetic" - which is the state
   where moving the arithmetic is the only lever left.

   🔴 **BUT IT IS THE SAME FUSED KERNEL ITEM 9 DECLINED**, because ESMFold2
   imports AF3's `src/af3/pair-track-gpu.js`. The fusion keeps the widened
   hidden activation out of global memory, and AF2's transition was a good
   target BECAUSE it materialises its hidden. So the question to answer FIRST
   is whether a staged matrix transition can keep the hidden in workgroup
   memory and stay fused; a naive split pays item 9's two intermediates and a
   pass. Do not start here on the share alone.

14. **MEASURED, AND THE PREMISE WAS WRONG - but the measurement found a
   different 508 MiB.** See "Where AF3's device memory actually is" below.
   Nothing about AF2's `releaseScratchSince` transfers: AF3's peak was never
   undisciplined pairformer scratch. It was the TEMPLATE EMBEDDER, and the fix
   is ordering rather than an API.

   **Scratch-release discipline for AF3 and ESMFold2 - measure before
   claiming.** `releaseScratchSince` in `WebGpuExecution` took AF2's peak from
   **11.3 to 7.2 GiB**, and `WebGpuExecution` is used by AF2 alone. AF3 and
   ESMFold2 allocate through `GpuBufferAllocator` without that lifetime
   discipline. Whether any of it transfers is unmeasured: `fold.js --budget=0`
   and `probe-memory.js` first.

15. **CLOSED - it forwards the object now.** `predict` reads the three keys it
   knows and ignores the rest, so the seam cannot go stale. What follows is
   what it was.

   **Latent, low: `AlphaFoldQueryOnlyGpu.predictSequence` still hand-copies
   three options** where `predictA3m` now forwards the object. It is not a live
   bug - the page dropped that driver, and its `predict` reads only the three
   it copies - but it is the same stale-list shape that cost the page its
   contact overlay. See docs/AF2.md.

**Closed, so nobody re-opens it:** the unfolded-dispatch class (a shader
indexing from `.x` under a grid that folds into y) was audited across all 157
`fn main` in every model. ESMFold2 is clean - 38 folding sites, zero `.x`-only
shaders. AF3 folds with its own `GRID_WIDTH`. `src/structure/ipa.js`'s folded
logits shader folds correctly, and its `.x`-only softmax is dispatched
unfolded and bounded by the 65535 throw. Only AF2's and the multimer's global
attention KV shaders were wrong, and both are fixed.


## The pairformer's cubic kernel on the matrix units

`src/af3/grid-attention-matrix.js`, which is item 11 above, built. It replaces
`grid.attend` - the largest pass in an AF3 pairformer and the only CUBIC one -
with the same online-softmax flash attention AF2's `attention-matrix.js` runs,
contracting sixteen queries against sixteen keys per instruction instead of
giving one invocation one query and having it read every key itself.

**One kernel, two models and three stacks.** OpenDDE runs AF3's pairformer, so
`src/af3/pairformer-block-webgpu.js` and `src/af3/msa-stack-webgpu.js` both take
it, at whatever head width the bundle has - `resolveGridAttendMatrix` reads
`pairAttention1.dimension` from the loaded block and answers false where the
device cannot hold that shape, rather than throwing. ESMFold2 imports the same
track and does NOT get it: `compilePairTrack`'s `gridAttention` is false there,
because an ESMFold2 block is a pairformer block with both grid attentions
removed. Item 13 is still where ESMFold2's time is.

**What it costs, in the trunk, which is the only place the answer is legible.**
`grid.attend` as GPU pass time over eight blocks at 32 MSA rows, medians of
three passes, arms in separate processes and reproducible to 0.15%:

    tokens        200     300     384     400     512     640
    scalar      17.16   45.24   90.71  108.02  208.06  405.85
    matrix 4x32 11.41   31.29   64.79   81.77  148.60  289.39
    matrix 2x16 11.23   34.31   71.01   74.57  165.36  321.44
    speedup      1.53    1.45    1.40    1.45    1.40    1.40

and the whole trunk's GPU time with it, 417.4 -> 383.7 ms at 400 tokens.

🔴 **AND AF2's TILE RULE DOES NOT GOVERN HERE, WHICH IS THE FINDING.** That
kernel's five geometries ranked EXACTLY by workgroup bytes a lane, and
docs/AF2.md states the rule as "every fixed cost such a kernel is built to
amortise is worth less than the bytes it takes to amortise it". These do not
rank by it at all. At 512 tokens:

    tile     bytes a lane   attend ms
    4x32              169       148.6
    4x16              136       162.4
    2x16              154       165.4
    6x16              130       166.1

The winner takes the MOST bytes a lane, and the two cheapest are the two
slowest. **AF2's kernel was occupancy-starved and this one is not**: a
pairformer pass launches n x heads workgroups per block - 16,384 of them at 512
tokens over eight blocks, against AF2's few hundred - so the device is full at
any of these tiles and what is left to win is the fixed cost a bigger tile
amortises, which is the opposite trade. Two rules, one per kernel, and the way
to tell which applies is the workgroup count.

🔴 **WHAT MOVES THE TILE HERE IS DIVISIBILITY, AND 400 IS WHERE IT SHOWS.**
4x32 wins four of the six lengths and loses 400 by 9.7%. 400/64 is 6.25, so the
row tile pays a seventh workgroup where 384 needs six, and the key tile is
ragged on top of it; 2x16's rows divide 400 more nearly. The default is 4x32 on
the strength of the other four, and `--tune=gridAttendMatrixTile=` is how to
re-ask on a device or a length distribution that is not this one.

**What it does to the answer.** The units multiply in f16 with an f32
accumulator and the probabilities go back through f16, so it is held to the f16
bar and not to the f32 kernel's:

    tools/gpu/check-grid-attend-matrix.js, matrix against the CPU reference
      head 32   9.8e-4 to 6.6e-3      head 16   9.8e-4 to 2.0e-2
      head  8   7.3e-4 to 1.4e-2      scalar f32, all three: about 1e-6

    tools/gpu/check-af3-block-any.js, two blocks at 40 tokens, pair relRMS
      staged f32, accumulate f32, scalar attend    1.9e-5
      ...with the matrix attend                    4.4e-3

and on a real 68-token fold, against the arm without it: **max |dx| 0.008 A,
4 of 574 atoms identical, mean pLDDT 84.20887 -> 84.20997**. For scale, the
`attnSplits` regrouping this repository already ships moves 0.001 A. Geometry
is unchanged to two decimals - N-CA 1.44, CA-C 1.53, CA-CA 3.82, radius of
gyration 11.1 A.

🔴 **AND THE HEAD WIDTHS THE OTHER TWO MODELS USE ARE CHECKED ON A BOX THAT HAS
ONLY AF3's WEIGHTS.** `check-grid-attend-matrix.js` synthesises weights of the
right SHAPES rather than loading a bundle, so OpenDDE's 8 and the template
embedder's 16 are gated here. It sweeps the token count across both tails as
well - the kernel emits a partial-key body only when the count does not divide
the key tile and a partial-row guard only when it does not divide the row tile,
so a single size checks at most one of the four combinations.

🔴 **THE MASK STAYED A CONDITIONAL SUBTRACTION, DELIBERATELY.** The scalar
kernel carries a measured warning against lifting it to an additive penalty -
relRMS 2.24e-1 against the CPU reference where the conditional measures 9.63e-7,
for an algebraically identical expression that was never run down. Only the
READ is hoisted here, out of the per-query-row loop it was in and into a staged
per-key array; the conditional itself is written exactly as it is there. A trap
that is recorded and not explained is a trap to route around.

### Two gates that were printing numbers and asserting on none

Item 12's shape, in the two places this work needed it.

`check-af3-block-any.js` printed a pair and a single relRMS and gated on
neither, so every arm it has ever been run with "passed". It gates now, and
**the bound follows the arm**, because one bound would stop checking the tight
path: the f16 triangle ACCUMULATOR is a recorded trade worth 8.3e-2 by itself,
the matrix attend 4.4e-3, and the f32/f32 scalar path reaches 1.9e-5. A single
bound wide enough for the first stops checking the third.

`bench-trunk.js` and `check-af3-block-any.js` both take `--tune=key=value` now,
the same flag `fold.js` carries. Neither could reach a device-profile knob
before, and `bench-trunk.js` is the only tool that can see what one costs
INSIDE the trunk - which is where a pairformer kernel's answer is legible and a
standalone dispatch's is not.


## Item 9 was right for AF3 and wrong for ESMFold2, and the reason is the width

Item 13 was blocked on item 9's question - whether a transition can be taken
off the fused kernel without paying the two intermediates the fusion exists to
avoid. Measured, the answer is different for the two models, and the difference
is one number.

**Where ESMFold2's trunk actually is**, 300 tokens, 24 blocks, staged f16, by
GPU pass time (`bench-esmfold2-trunk.js --tokens=300 --precision=f16
--profile`):

    pair-transition       419.88 ms   56.5%
    tri.project           130.36      17.5
    tri.project-out       106.45      14.3
    tri.contract           63.95       8.6
    tri.normalize-hidden    12.30       1.7
    tri.normalize          10.89       1.5

One kernel is more than the other five together, and docs/EF2FAST.md's 47-50%
was if anything conservative.

**The same transition, three ways.** The fused kernel is
`tools/gpu/bench-transition.js`; the split is `bench-evoformer-linear.js` on
the two halves as plain GEMMs, best arm of each:

    ESMFold2   90,000 rows, 256 channels, factor 4
      fused, best tile (4)                          16.07 ms
      two vector GEMMs, f16                          7.82      2.05x
      two staged matrix GEMMs, f16 vector staging    5.07      3.17x

    AF3        40,000 rows, 128 channels, factor 4
      fused, best tile (16:128)                      1.138 ms
      two vector GEMMs, f16                          1.134     1.00x
      two staged matrix GEMMs                        0.794     1.43x

🔴 **SO SPLITTING IS A WASH AT 128 CHANNELS AND 2x AT 256, BEFORE ANY MATRIX
UNITS ARE INVOLVED.** Item 9 declined the split on the strength of the two
intermediates it would add, and at AF3's width that is the right call - there
is nothing to win to pay for them. At ESMFold2's width the fused kernel is
already losing to two plain GEMMs by a factor of two.

🔴 **AND THE MECHANISM IS THE THING THE FUSION IS FOR.** What the fused kernel
holds in workgroup memory is the WIDENED row: `2 * channels * factor` floats.
At 128 channels that is 4 KB and the row tile is 16; at 256 it is 8 KB and the
tile collapses to 4, taking with it the weight-read amortisation the tile
exists to buy - 8:128 measures 21.2 ms against tile 4's 16.07, and 16:256 is
36.2. Per row: AF3 28.5 ns, ESMFold2 178.6 ns for exactly four times the work,
which is **1.57x worse per multiply-add**. The fusion's cost scales with the
channel count and its benefit does not, so there is a width at which it stops
paying and 256 is past it.

**What that is worth.** 419.88 -> about 143 ms on the trunk's largest kernel,
which is 744 -> 467 ms of GPU, **1.59x on an ESMFold2 trunk** - and the trunk
is 85% of a 300-token fold. It is the largest single item left in this file.

### Built, and what it moved

Three passes, wired behind `pairTransitionSplit` and declined below 192
channels. ESMFold2's trunk at 300 tokens, 24 blocks, staged f16:

                             fused     split
    pair-transition        419.88    169.83 ms    2.47x
    trunk, GPU time        743.80    494.00       1.51x
    trunk, wall           1023.1     774.5        1.32x
    peak device memory      445.8     517.8 MiB

The 72 MiB is the widened activation, chunked: `transitionSplitChunkRows` takes
16,384 rows at a time and the pair is bound at a row offset per chunk, so it
does not scale with the protein. The kernel's 2.47x is under the synthetic
2.89x by the chunking and the real weight layout.

**AF3 declines it, and that is the width rule doing its job.** At 128 channels
and 400 tokens it is 57.59 -> 48.45 ms on the kernel and **390.7 -> 383.9 of a
trunk**, 1.8%, for the same 72 MiB. `TRANSITION_SPLIT_MIN_CHANNELS` is 192 and
carries the table.

**OpenDDE is 3.71x on the kernel and a wash on a 68-residue fold**, which is
not a contradiction: at 68 tokens the pair track is 4,624 rows and the whole
fold is 200 diffusion steps. 23945 -> 23779 ms, pLDDT 92.0497 -> 92.0503. Its
384 channels are why the kernel number is the largest of the three, and a long
OpenDDE chain is where that shows.

**What it did to the answers**, every gate on this box:

    check-transition-split.js  6.0e-4 against the fused kernel, chunked and not
    check-af3-block-any.js     4.425e-3 -> 4.445e-3 (the matrix attend's error)
    fold.js                    pLDDT 85.8 -> 85.9, geometry identical
    fold-opendde.js            CA-CA 3.67058 -> 3.67053, pLDDT 92.0497 -> 92.0503
    fold-esmfold2.js           CA-CA median 3.80468 -> 3.80483, contact map identical

**And the next kernel is visible in the profile now.** With the transition off
the top, ESMFold2's trunk is `tri.project` 130.6 ms and `tri.project-out` 106.5
of 494 - 48% between them, both GEMM-shaped, both still on the vector path.

### The shape of the build, as it was reasoned before it was built

Three passes, not two. The first GEMM's epilogue would
have to pair column `i` with column `i + hidden` to fuse the swish gate, and
those are 1024 columns apart - different workgroups at any block width - so the
gate is its own elementwise pass. It reads 2*hidden and writes hidden, 553 MB
at 300 tokens, about 0.36 ms against an 11 ms saving. Both intermediates are
chunked over rows the way `transitionChunkRows` already chunks AF2's; that
kernel is the template, because AF2's transition is the one this repository
has already taken off its fused form and onto `createStagedMatrixShader`.


## Where AF3's device memory actually is, which is not where item 14 looked

Item 14 proposed carrying AF2's `releaseScratchSince` discipline - which took
its peak from 11.3 to 7.2 GiB - to AF3 and ESMFold2, and said to measure first.
Measured, at 400 tokens over 8 pairformer blocks with 4 template slots
(`bench-trunk.js`, which reports `deviceMemory`):

    peak 575.0 MiB, and the labels holding it at that moment:
      af3-template.scratch    195.3 MiB   five pair-sized, CHANNELS = 64
      af3-template.pair        78.1
      af3-template.output      78.1
      af3-template.readback    78.1
      af3-template.act         39.1
      af3-template.summed      39.1

**508 MiB of a 575 MiB trunk is one stage, and it is not the pairformer** - the
pairformer's own weights are 66 MiB of that peak. So the item as written was
looking in the wrong place, and the pairformer's scratch discipline is fine.

🔴 **AND IT IS AN ORDERING PROBLEM, NOT A MISSING RELEASE.** The embedder
allocated every buffer up front and freed them in one `finally`, so its five
pair-sized scratch tensors - dead the moment the last slot accumulates -
coexisted with `output` and `readback`, which are not needed until after that.
Nothing was leaking; two sets of tensors that never overlap in use were
overlapping in time.

🔴 **AND THE SUBMIT IS WHAT MAKES THE RELEASE LEGAL, which is the part that is
not obvious.** This allocator does not pool, so `release()` destroys - and
destroying a buffer that an encoded-but-unsubmitted pass still names is a
use-after-free WebGPU reports at submit time and not before. The stage is one
command buffer, so the scratch cannot be released inside it. It is two now: the
slot loop and its blocks, then a submit and the release, then the output
projection and its copy.

    peak                575.0 -> 555.3 MiB
    template stage        316 ->   245 ms   (three runs: 248, 247, 244)
    trunk, whole         1496 ->  1435
    fold                pLDDT 85.8, geometry identical

The peak falls by less than the stage does, because removing the template as
the peak just promotes whatever was second: **the MSA stack's five pair-sized
scratch tensors, 390.6 MiB at 400 tokens**, which is now the top holder in the
trunk and the next place to look. The template stage getting 22% faster is
reproducible across three runs and is not explained here; it is not the reason
for the change.

**ESMFold2 needs none of this.** It allocates one set of scratch for the whole
24-block loop and releases it after, which is the discipline item 14 was
proposing to add. Its peak at 300 tokens is 445.8 MiB, or 517.8 with the split
transition's chunked intermediate.


## The triangle projection on the units, and the width rule that keeps it off AF3

With the transition split, `tri.project` was the top of an ESMFold2 trunk's
profile - 130.6 ms of 494. It is a dense projection over a materialised source,
which is the shape docs/A100.md's GEMM rule actually governs; the kernel that
rule ruled out is `tri.contract`, whose K is the protein's length.

🔴 **AND IT NEEDS NO NEW MEMORY, WHICH THE TRANSITION'S SPLIT DID.** Its source
is the layer-normed pair and its two outputs are `a` and `b` - all three
pair-sized scratch the track already holds. Nothing is materialised that was
not materialised before.

🔴 **THE FOUR MATRICES ARE ONE GEMM WITH INTERLEAVED COLUMNS.** `projectAB`
contracts a, a's gate, b and b's gate over one read of the source and gates them
pairwise. A staged matmul's epilogue can only reach the columns inside its own
subgroup's flush block, so the four roles of channel `h` are packed as columns
`4h..4h+3` - always inside one block, whatever the geometry, because four
divides every flush width this uses. `AB_INTERLEAVED` in
src/triangle/weights.js transposes and interleaves in one pass at pack time and
**replaces** the four separate matrices, so a bundle costs no extra bytes and
only one of the two kernels is compiled for a block. The store stays
channel-major, because `tri.contract` reads it that way.

    tri.project over 8 blocks    vector    matrix   speedup
      128 channels (AF3)          33.96     31.01      1.10
      256 channels (ESMFold2)    130.55     85.59      1.53

    ESMFold2 trunk, GPU:  743.8 -> 494.0 (the split) -> 448.8   1.66x total

🔴 **IT COSTS ACCURACY, AND THE UNITS ARE WHY.** The units multiply BOTH
operands in f16, where the vector kernel's f16 mode narrows only the staged
activation and keeps the weights in f32. On `check-af3-block-any.js`'s
uniform-noise input, one block at 40 tokens:

    vector, staged f32                     1.4e-5
    vector, staged f16 (the shipped path)  2.7e-3
    matrix                                 2.0e-2

flat in the token count - 2.2e-2 at 16, 1.6e-2 at 64 - so it is the operand
rounding and not the contraction's length. There is no mitigation: this device
offers no f32 matrix configuration at all.

🔴 **SO IT IS OFF BELOW 192 CHANNELS, AND THE FOLDS ARE WHAT JUSTIFY IT ABOVE.**
At AF3's 128 it is 0.7% of a trunk for that error and the default declines it -
the same shape of rule `TRANSITION_SPLIT_MIN_CHANNELS` carries, for a different
reason: that one trades memory and this one trades precision. Where it does
apply, the block figure is not the evidence - that input is uniform noise,
which docs/AF3.md already records as harsher than a real pair representation -
the folds are:

    ESMFold2   CA-CA median 3.80480 -> 3.80483, contact precision 1.000 either way
    OpenDDE    CA-CA 3.6705 -> 3.6708, pLDDT 92.0503 -> 92.0508
    AlphaFold 3   declines it; pLDDT 85.8 and geometry identical either way

🔴 **AND THE TWO KNOBS WERE BRIEFLY ONE, WHICH IS ITS OWN LESSON.** Deriving
`triangleProjectMatrix` from the transition's config made it silently inert
whenever `pairTransitionSplit` was false - which is exactly the arm a checker
isolating the two reaches for, and it reads as "the kernel changes nothing"
rather than as "the flag did nothing". Three arms of a sweep returned the
identical figure to sixteen digits before that was noticed.

### And the output projection, which is two GEMMs and not one

`tri.project-out` is `logistic(z . Wg) * (x . Wz)` - two contractions over two
DIFFERENT sources, the layer-normed pair and the normalised contraction. No
column trick makes that one GEMM: interleaving, which is what worked for a/b,
would still need both sources staged at once.

🔴 **SO THE GATE IS ITS OWN PASS AND THE PROJECTION READS IT BACK.** Two staged
sources with two accumulator sets fits the workgroup budget and then spends
about 128 registers a lane on the accumulators; `outputModulate` - one global
read at the output cell the store is about to write - costs no registers at
all. And it needs no new memory either: at that point in the track `scratch[2]`
and `scratch[3]` are both dead, so the gate lands in one of them.

    tri.project-out over 24 blocks       vector    matrix
      fused                              106.47
      gate + modulated projection                  63.92   1.67x
        (25.67 gate, 38.25 projection)

    ESMFold2 trunk, GPU:  743.8 -> 494.0 -> 448.8 -> 406.0     1.83x

Its two matrices are stored output-channel-major and a staged matmul reads
K x N, so the same pack that interleaves a/b transposes these - a reshape, same
element count, done once at pack time.

**Checked** against the vector kernel at relRMS 1.1e-4 (256 channels) and
1.3e-4 (384), in its RESIDUAL form from a non-zero target, because a residual
that writes agrees with one that adds whenever the target starts at zero. The
folds are unchanged: ESMFold2 CA-CA median 3.80502 with contact precision
1.000, OpenDDE 3.6708 at pLDDT 92.0507, AF3 85.8 and geometry identical.

### The geometry those four share, which had never been swept in situ

At 406 ms the top four kernels were within 8% of each other and all four were
staged matrix GEMMs on the SAME block - 128x128x32 with 2x4 subgroups, carried
over from the sweep in `bench-evoformer-linear.js`, which measures a GEMM as a
standalone dispatch. In the trunk the answer is different. `stagedMatrixBlock`
makes it a knob; total GPU time of an ESMFold2 trunk at 300 tokens:

    64x128x16x1x8    349.8      128x128x16x2x4   386.7
    64x128x16x1x4    364.8      64x128x32x1x8    388.0
    64x64x16x1x4     373.8      128x128x32x2x4   405.4   the old default
    128x128x16x1x8   386.7      32x128x16x1x8    416.4
                                256x128x16x1x8   503.2
                                128x256x16x1x8   487.6

**1.16x for a knob**, and every one of the four kernels improves at the winner.

🔴 **TWO THINGS MOVE IT AND THEY PULL THE SAME WAY.** A smaller K panel - 16
beat 32 and 64 at every block, which is what `matrix-linear.js`'s own note
already said and this confirms in situ - and fewer accumulators a lane: 64x128
over eight subgroups gives a lane FOUR results where 128x128 over 2x4 gives it
eight. Both are occupancy, which is the same axis `grid.attend` was NOT on. Two
matrix kernels in this file, opposite answers to "does a bigger tile pay", and
the discriminator is the workgroup count.

🔴 **AND IT IS THE THIRD TIME A STANDALONE BENCH GAVE THE WRONG TILE.** CLAUDE.md
already says to measure a kernel in the stack it runs in; the block that won
`bench-evoformer-linear.js --shape=ef2wide` is 16% off in the trunk. The
checkers follow the shipped block now (`--block=` overrides), so what is
checked is what runs.

**Where an ESMFold2 trunk is now**, GPU time at 300 tokens over 24 blocks:

    original                                  743.8 ms
    + the split transition                    494.0    1.51x
    + the matrix triangle projection          448.8    1.66x
    + the matrix output projection            406.0    1.83x
    + the swept block                         349.8    2.13x

    wall                                     1023.1 -> 659.6    1.55x
    peak                                      445.8 -> 517.8 MiB

### The cubic kernel, which this file's GEMM rule did not rule out after all

`tri.contract` is `out[h][i][j] = sum_k a[h][i][k] * b[h][j][k]` - one n x n by
n x n product PER CHANNEL, 256 of them, all the same shape and the same stride.
It is a BATCHED GEMM, and the batch is the dimension the vector kernel already
dispatches over.

🔴 **THE RULE SAID "THE INNER EXTENT MUST DIVIDE BY FOUR" AND THIS FILE READ IT
AS "SO THE UNITS CANNOT HELP HERE".** The inner extent is the protein's length,
which at 825 is not divisible by four, and the measured 0.95x was recorded and
believed. What the rule actually constrains is ONE OPERAND: the transposed one
walks the axis it is not stored on, so its four consecutive elements are
`columns` apart and it cannot be vec4-read. **The other one still can.** Built
with `vectorStaging` off for both, exactly as the rule implies, it is a wash:

    tri.contract over 24 blocks    vector 63.94    matrix, both scalar  64.24
                                                   matrix, one vec4     47.82   1.34x

So `vectorStaging` is per operand now, and the two directions take one each -
outgoing transposes the RIGHT operand and incoming the LEFT, and swapping them
returns a finite tensor of exactly the same shape, which is why the checker has
an arm for each and fails at 3.8e-1 when they are crossed.

**Where an ESMFold2 trunk ends up**, GPU time at 300 tokens over 24 blocks:

    original                                  743.8 ms
    + the split transition                    494.0    1.51x
    + the matrix triangle projection          448.8    1.66x
    + the matrix output projection            406.0    1.83x
    + the swept block                         349.8    2.13x
    + the batched contraction                 332.8    2.23x

    wall                                     1023.1 -> 650.5   1.57x
    peak                                      445.8 -> 517.8 MiB

and every kernel in the pair track is now on the units except three layer
norms totalling 31 ms. The profile is `tri.project` 72.2,
`pair-transition.wide` 72.2, `pair-transition.down` 57.8, `tri.contract` 47.8,
`tri.project-out` 30.5, `tri.project-out.gate` 21.3.

🔴 **AND THAT IS THREE TIMES THIS FILE'S OWN GEMM RULE HAS BEEN MISREAD IN ONE
DIRECTION.** It ruled out AF2's flash attention (wrong: a flash attention's
reuse is in its loop, not in K), AF3's fused transitions (wrong above 128
channels: the fusion's cost scales with the width), and this contraction
(wrong: the constraint is per operand). The rule is about a GEMM's staging and
it is correct about that; every misreading has been of what it implies for a
kernel that is not a plain GEMM. **Read it as a statement about one operand's
memory access, not as a verdict on a kernel.**


## The three layer norms, and what is actually left

With everything else on the units, an ESMFold2 trunk's remaining non-matrix work
is three layer norms - 31 ms of 332.8, or 9.4%. **They cannot go on the units at
all**: a layer norm is a row REDUCTION, not a contraction, and there is no hook
in a staged matmul that can see a whole row (which is also why the split
transition's normalise had to come out as its own pass). So the only question is
whether they are at bandwidth. Priced against this card's 1555 GB/s:

    tri.normalize                184.3 MB x48   10.86 ms    815 GB/s   52%
    tri.normalize-hidden         184.3 MB x48   12.25        722       46%
    pair-transition.normalize    138.2 MB x24    8.13        408       26%

🔴 **AND THE ODD ONE OUT WAS THE ONE THIS SESSION WROTE.** Two candidate causes
were obvious and both were wrong, which is the reason to write them down:

- **The reduction shape.** The transition's normalise reduced eight partials
  with a log tree - three barriers, twice over, once for the sum and once for
  the squares. The triangle's beside it writes both partials, barriers ONCE and
  has lane zero add eight numbers. Rewritten to match: **8.13 -> 10.05 ms**,
  24% WORSE. The tree is the right structure for this kernel and the serial sum
  is the right one for the triangle's, and nothing here explains why.
- **The half-word store.** The triangle's writeback owns a PAIR of channels per
  lane "so it owns the whole word they share"; this one wrote single f16
  elements, two lanes to a word. Paired: **8.13 -> 7.77 ms**, 4.4%. Real, and
  kept, and a twentieth of the gap.

🔴 **WHAT IT WAS IS THE CHUNKING, AND THE FIX TRADES WALL TIME FOR GPU TIME.**
The split transition walks the rows in chunks so its widened activation stays
bounded, and a chunk is its own dispatch: 16,384 rows is 2,048 workgroups of the
normalise, **59% of what this card holds**, six times a block. Raising the
budget (`pairTransitionChunkBytes`, in MiB):

    budget   chunk rows   normalise   wide   down    trunk GPU   wall    peak
     64 MiB      16,384        7.69   72.2   57.6        332.4  657.2   517.8 MiB
    128           32,768        6.29   70.5   46.2        318.0  667.9   589.8
    192           49,152        5.78   69.8   42.6        313.0  662.5   661.8
    512           90,000        5.14   69.2   40.9        310.0      -   see peak

The `down` pass gains most - 29% - because its dispatch is the smallest. **But
the wall does not see it**: three runs an arm give 657.2 ms at 64 MiB and 662.5
at 192, so a 6% GPU win is a 1% wall LOSS for 144 MiB. The trunk at this size is
not GPU-bound end to end, and creating 144 MiB more of buffers per recycle costs
more than the kernels save. The default stays at 64 and the knob is there for a
device with room and a longer chain.

🔴 **AND PACKING THEM IS A CLOSED QUESTION, NOT AN UNTRIED ONE.** Halving these
tensors would halve both the write and every downstream read - and
src/af3/pair-track-gpu.js has the measured table: `normalized`, `a` and `b`
packed cost the confidence head a factor of 1200 and miss the MSA block's bound
by 50x. Do not reopen it.

### Tried and refused: storing `b` transposed for the contraction

`tri.contract` is 47.8 ms at 13.9 TFLOP/s where the GEMMs beside it reach 26-31,
and the reason is that `b` is the one operand neither direction can vec4-read.
A diagnostic arm - the right memory pattern and the wrong answer, which is what
`bench-transition.js`'s `--diagnose` arms are for - priced both operands
vectorised at **33.6 ms**, a further 1.42x.

`b` is written by `tri.project` and read by nothing else, and that epilogue's
stores are strided by the channel whichever index they use - so writing it at
the transposed pair index looked free. Built, and it is not:

    tri.contract    47.82 -> 33.63    exactly what the diagnostic said
    tri.project     72.21 -> 88.33
    trunk           332.7 -> 334.6    a wash, slightly worse

The projection's store loses 16.1 ms where the contraction gains 14.2. A
transpose PASS is worse still: 184 MB a dispatch, 48 of them, about 11 ms of
bandwidth to save 14.2. Both are recorded here rather than left as an obvious
thing to try. The 14.2 ms is real and unclaimed; what would collect it is a
`tri.project` whose epilogue writes coalesced, which is a different kernel.

## Two thirds of the staging was a detour, and the probe said so

`tools/gpu/probe-staged-gemm-parts.js` neuters one region of the staged GEMM at
a time. At `ef2wide`'s shape - 90000 x 256 -> 2048, the shipped 64x128x16x1x8
block:

| arm | ms | TFLOP/s |
|---|---:|---:|
| full | 4.583 | 20.6 |
| no epilogue | 4.062 | 23.2 |
| **stage the panels, never multiply** | **3.104** | 30.4 |
| multiply out of uninitialised memory | 1.686 | 56.0 |

**The staging loop is 3.10 ms of a 4.58 ms kernel and the matrix issue is
1.69.** So the units are not what this kernel is short of - it is short of a way
to feed them, which is the shape of finding that says look at the feeding.

🔴 **AND THE PROBE HAD BEEN REPORTING 0.000 ms ON EVERY ARM.** It requested its
own GPUDevice off a fresh adapter, and a second device is handed out LOST here:
buffers, pipelines and submits all succeed as no-ops, `resolveQuerySet` writes
nothing, and the timestamp pair subtracts to exactly zero. Nothing throws. Four
identical zeroes were returned as a result and nobody read them as a failure.
It takes the runner's device now, which already asks for every feature and the
raised workgroup-storage limit that private device existed for. The arms that
then appeared were real errors: `layout: "auto"` drops a binding the neutered
shader stopped reading, so each arm binds only what its own layout asks for.

### Where the staging goes, and why the weights' share of it is free

A workgroup stages `BM x BK` of the source and `BK x BN` of the weights - at
64x128x16 that is 1024 halves against 2048, two thirds of it the weights. What
each buys is not the same:

- **The source panel is shared.** With `subgroupRows` 1 every subgroup has
  `sg_row` 0 and reads the same rows, so one staged panel feeds eight
  subgroups.
- **The weight panel is not.** Subgroup `sg` owns columns `sg * BN/SGX ..`,
  which no other subgroup touches. It is staged once and read once.

So the swept block's weight staging has a reuse factor of ONE, and the
workgroup memory is a pure detour. `subgroupMatrixLoad` takes a storage
pointer, so the tile can come out of the weight buffer at stride
`parameters.columns` - the same layout the staged panel has, at a different
stride. `directWeights` in src/runtime/matrix-linear.js is that.

    standalone, ef2wide            4.583 -> 3.190 ms       1.44x
    ESMFold2 trunk, 300 tokens, GPU  332.8 -> 296.2         1.12x
    ...wall                          675.6 -> 596.9         1.13x

Every one of the five staged GEMMs improves 11-17%: `pair-transition.wide`
70.75 -> 62.83, `tri.project` 70.62 -> 62.51, `pair-transition.down` 56.01 ->
47.57, `tri.project-out` 30.26 -> 26.95, its gate 20.80 -> 18.29.

🔴 **IT IS BIT-IDENTICAL, AND THREE CHECKERS SAY SO.**
`check-staged-matrix.js` gained the arm (192 cases now, relRMS 0 at every one);
`check-transition-split.js` reads 6.73e-4 staged and 6.73e-4 direct;
`check-triangle-project-matrix.js` reads 2.49e-4 both ways. A direct read forms
its own global offset from `weight_offset`, the panel and the block origin
where the staged form read a workgroup panel at a different stride, so an
off-by-one there is a plausible tensor rather than an error - which is why it is
three checkers and not the trunk's timing.

🔴 **AND IT NEEDS THE WEIGHT BUFFER TO HOLD HALVES, WHICH IS WHERE IT STOPS.**
There is no staging copy left to convert in. `directWeightsAllowed` is the gate
and it has three conditions: the buffer already holds the matrix element, the
right operand is not transposed, and the contracted extent divides by the K
panel - the buffer is bound as `[weights | bias]`, so a tail panel would read
the bias as a weight row.

### Which is why AF3 and OpenDDE get nothing from it

Wired into the pairformer's `matrixTile` and swept on OpenDDE at 256 tokens,
every pass is unchanged to within 0.3%: `tri.project` 245.42 against 245.62,
`pair-transition.wide` 195.76 against 195.94, the whole trunk 2119.3 against
2118.5. Instrumenting `directWeightsAllowed` says why in one line - the pair
track's weights are **f32**, and deliberately:
`pairWeightPrecision` defaults to f32 in src/af3/pairformer-block-webgpu.js
because narrowing them takes the block checker's pair from 17x its rounding
envelope to 51x and the confidence head's pLDDT from 3.0e-4 to 2.3e-2, for 38
MiB. That refusal stands and this does not reopen it.

🔴 **BUT THE REASON DOES NOT COVER THE MATRIX KERNELS, AND THAT IS THE OPEN
PART.** The measurement that set `pairWeightPrecision` was taken where the pair
weights feed VECTOR kernels, which multiply what they read. A staged matrix GEMM
rounds its weights to halves on the way in whatever the buffer holds, so for
`tri.project`, `tri.project-out` and the transition's two projections an f16
buffer is the same arithmetic at half the traffic - and the amplification is
paid only by the tensors the matrix path does NOT read, which in the packed
block is the four layer-norm scale/offset pairs. Separating those is a buffer
split, not a knob, and it is not done here.

### What it costs the ESMFold2 trunk to opt in, which is where it can be taken

That trunk's `weightPrecision` is its own option, defaulting to f32, and
`--weights=f16` on `bench-esmfold2-trunk.js` is the arm. On its own the buffer
narrowing is worth 332.8 -> 326.5 ms of GPU and 675.6 -> 622.7 of wall - the
staging reads mostly hit L2, so halving them is small; what it buys is the
direct read above. What it costs:

| | relRMS against the reference kernel |
|---|---|
| `check-triangle-project-matrix.js`, f32 weights | 2.00e-4 |
| ...f16 weights | 2.49e-4 |
| `check-transition-split.js`, f32 weights | 6.01e-4 |
| ...f16 weights | 6.73e-4 |

...and end to end, `fold-esmfold2.js --trunk-weights=f16
--tune=stagedMatrixDirectWeights=true` against the default, same seed:

| | max CA displacement | mean | certainty | contact recall |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 40 tokens | 0.009 A | 0.004 | 0.7924 -> 0.7924 | 0.628 -> 0.628 |
| 1QYS, 91 tokens | 0.241 A | 0.125 | 0.7437 -> 0.7417 | 0.494 -> 0.494 |

🔴 **AND THE ORACLE IS THE ONE GATE THAT COULD NOT BE RUN.**
`check-esmfold2-trunk-gpu.js` wants `oracle-dumps/esmfold2-trunk-40.json`, which
is not on this box - `tools/esmc/dump-esmfold2-trunk.py` needs a torch
environment this machine does not have. Everything above compares this
repository against itself. **The default is unchanged for that reason**; the
knob and the option are both wired, both checked, and both off. On a machine
with the dump, the arm to run is
`check-esmfold2-trunk-gpu.js --weights=f16 --tune=stagedMatrixDirectWeights=true`,
which is why those two flags exist there.

## The accumulator is a REGISTER knob, and it is worth 1.19x

`probe-matrix-ceiling.js` says f32 accumulation is free - 309.7 TFLOP/s into f32
against 310.9 into f16 - and that is true of the UNITS and false of the kernel
around them. Four `subgroup_matrix_result<f32, 16, 16>` are 32 registers a lane
before anything else, and eight workgroups of 256 threads need 32 registers a
thread in total to fill this card. So the accumulator's width is what the
occupancy this kernel is bound by is spent on.

`stagedMatrixResult` narrows it. ESMFold2 trunk, 300 tokens, on top of the
direct weight read:

    f32 accumulators   295.9 ms      f16 accumulators   253.1 ms   1.17x

    pair-transition.wide   62.8 -> 51.1     tri.project-out   26.9 -> 24.0
    pair-transition.down   47.6 -> 35.0     ...its gate       18.3 -> 15.4
    tri.project            62.4 -> 49.6     tri.contract      47.8 -> 47.8

🔴 **AND IT IS OCCUPANCY AND NOT ARITHMETIC, WHICH THE BLOCK SWEEP CONFIRMS FROM
THE OTHER SIDE.** Re-swept with the direct read on, because CLAUDE.md's rule is
that a tile's optimum moves when the body does - and here it does not move at
all: 64x128x16x1x8 296.2, 128x128x16x2x8 310.5, 64x256x16x1x8 331.2,
128x128x16x1x8 346.6, 128x128x16x2x4 358.1, 128x128x32x1x8 384.4,
128x256x16x2x4 498.8. Every geometry that gives a lane more results is worse,
in order. Four subgroups' worth of accumulators is what this card has room for,
and the only way to have more of them is to make each one narrower.

An earlier sweep at a CONSTANT four accumulators a lane - more subgroups per
workgroup rather than more results per lane, which nothing had tried - says the
same: 64x128x16x1x8 333.1, 128x128x16x2x8 329.5, 64x256x16x1x16 365.2,
256x128x16x4x8 392.3, 128x256x16x2x16 385.3. One percent, at best.

### The rule: narrow where K is a channel count, not where it is the protein

🔴 **APPLIED TO ALL FIVE STAGED GEMMs IT RETURNS 2178 NaN COORDINATES.** The
fold does not degrade, it fails - and the two differential checkers pass the
whole time, at 2.19e-3 and 6.25e-4, because their operands are small
deterministic patterns that never approach f16's 65504. Bisected by folding 1QYS
one kernel group at a time:

| narrowed | NaN | certainty |
|---|---:|---|
| the transition's two projections | 0 | 0.7419 |
| the triangle's three projections | 0 | 0.7419 |
| **`tri.contract` alone** | **2178** | - |

`tri.contract` is `sum_k a[h][i][k] * b[h][j][k]`: its K is the PROTEIN'S LENGTH
where every other staged GEMM in the track contracts a channel count, and its
two operands are gated projections multiplied against each other, so its partial
sums are the only ones not bounded by a width. That is the same distinction the
outer product mean's f16 contraction draws, and the same shape of threshold -
fine at one size, inf at another.

So the contraction keeps the device config's own result type whatever the knob
says (`contractResult` in src/af3/pair-track-gpu.js), and
`check-triangle-project-matrix.js` follows the same rule with its own
`--contract-result=`, because an arm that narrowed all four would be checking a
configuration nothing may ship.

### OpenDDE takes it too, and its fold does not notice

The accumulator's width is independent of the weight buffer's, so the knob
reaches AF3's pairformer where the direct read could not - and OpenDDE runs that
pairformer at 384 channels, where both matrix kernels are on. At 256 tokens,
`bench-trunk.js --model=/model-opendde-int5/manifest.json`:

| | f32 | f16 |
|---|---:|---:|
| whole trunk | 2118.1 ms | **1945.7** |
| `tri.project` | 245.1 | 207.9 |
| `pair-transition.wide` | 196.3 | 159.2 |
| `pair-transition.down` | 194.8 | 128.2 |
| `tri.contract` | (unchanged - it keeps f32 by the rule above) | |

**1.09x**, and the fold is the same fold: `fold-opendde.js --target=6mrr` gives
RMSD 1.655 -> 1.654 against the crystal, TM 0.8845 -> 0.8846, CA-CA 3.6708 ->
3.6710, pLDDT 92.0507 -> 92.0508.

🔴 **AND THE BLOCK CHECKER IS WHERE THE PRICE SHOWS, WHICH IS THE POINT OF
HAVING IT.** `check-af3-block-any.js --model=/model-opendde-int5/manifest.json`
reads pair 1.23e-2 at f32 and **4.18e-2** at f16, against a bound of 1.5e-1 -
3.4x, the same factor the two ESMFold2 differentials report. A fold that does
not move and a differential that triples is exactly the pattern
`pairWeightPrecision` was declined over, and it is why this is a knob.

AF3 itself is untouched: at 128 pair channels both matrix kernels are below
their width rules and the staged GEMM is not in that trunk at all.

### Where the ESMFold2 trunk stands, and what is still ungated

    original                                743.8 ms
    + the split transition                  494.0    1.51x
    + the matrix triangle projection        448.8    1.66x
    + the matrix output projection          406.0    1.83x
    + the swept block                       349.8    2.13x
    + the batched contraction               332.8    2.23x
    + f16 weights and the direct read       295.9    2.51x
    + f16 accumulators, not the contraction 253.1    **2.94x**

    wall                                   1023.1 -> 584.0    1.75x

Folding 1QYS at 91 tokens against the default, same seed: max CA displacement
0.121 A, mean 0.059, certainty 0.7437 -> 0.7432, contact recall unchanged at
0.494, CA-CA median 3.8021 -> 3.8025.

🔴 **BOTH KNOBS ARE OFF BY DEFAULT AND THE REASON IS THE SAME ONE.**
`stagedMatrixResult: "f16"` costs the transition's differential 6.8e-4 -> 2.19e-3
and the triangle's 2.49e-4 -> 6.25e-4, which is the same order as the
`accumulatePrecision: "f16"` arm that this repository already offers and already
declines to default (2.68e-3 on the trunk). Deciding it needs
`check-esmfold2-trunk-gpu.js` against `oracle-dumps/esmfold2-trunk-40.json`, and
that dump is not on this box.

🔴 **AND `stagedMatrixResult` IS THE MATRIX PATH'S `accumulatePrecision`, WHICH
MEANS THAT KNOB HAS BEEN HALF-INERT HERE.** `accumulatePrecision: "f16"` narrows
the VECTOR triangle projection's vec4 accumulators; on a device that takes the
matrix path those kernels are gone, and the staged GEMM's result type came from
the device config instead. So the f16:f16 arm this repository measures at
2.68e-3 was, on this card, f16 tiles with f32 matrix accumulators. The two
should probably be one knob; they are not yet, and saying so is cheaper than
changing the meaning of an arm that has numbers attached to it.

## AF2 takes both knobs and barely moves, which says where its time is

Wired into `chooseMatrixLinear` (the transition) and the outer product mean's
own geometry builder, with the same rule: the OPM's CONTRACTION keeps f32
because its K is the alignment depth, and its output projection may narrow
because its K is `c_outer^2`. At 400 residues, 512/1024, warm:

| | warm | checksum |
|---|---:|---|
| neither | 4.421 s | -2887936 |
| the direct weight read | 4.351 | **-2887936, identical** |
| ...and f16 accumulators | 4.323 | -2869529 |

**1.6% and 2.2%**, against 12% and 19% on an ESMFold2 trunk. The direct read is
free here in every sense - `chooseLinearKernel` already returns
`weightPrecision: "f16"` on the matrix path, so the buffer is halves already,
and the fold's atom checksum does not move by one.

🔴 **AND THE SMALL NUMBER IS THE FINDING.** Profiled per dispatch at that shape,
a block is 78.01 ms and only 14.8 of it is a staged matrix GEMM:

| | ms | share | on the units? |
|---|---:|---:|---|
| the four flash attentions | 18.75 | 24% | yes |
| q/k/v/gate projections | 15.35 | 20% | **no - fused vector** |
| transitions | 14.81 | 19% | yes, staged |
| outer product mean | 10.75 | 14% | yes, staged |
| triangle multiplication | 9.51 | 12% | no |
| attention output projections | 6.55 | 8% | no |

So AF2's remaining time is in the kernels these two knobs cannot reach, and the
next thing to move is the 28% that is still a vector projection.

### What a matrix q/k/v/gate would be worth, priced before writing it

The projection is 204800 x 256 -> 4 x 256 at this shape, fused: one source read,
four matrices, 16.9 TFLOP/s. `probe-staged-gemm-parts.js` at the PACKED shape -
one GEMM of 204800 x 256 -> 1024:

    staged                        5.347 ms   20.1 TFLOP/s
    + the direct weight read      3.696      29.1
    + f16 accumulators            3.704      29.0   (four accumulators already)

against the fused kernel's 6.364. **1.72x**, or 5.3 ms of a 78 ms block, 6.8%.

Two things make it more tractable than it sounds and one makes it less:

- **The weights are already interleaved the way the matrix path reads them.**
  `createAttentionProjectShader` packs the four matrices as one vec4 per
  (channel, output) cell, which is `[k][4h + role]` - exactly the layout
  `outputGroup` was built for on AF3's triangle projection.
- **f16 weights are free here and already measured**: the note in that file
  prices them at 9.762 -> 9.238 ms with BITWISE IDENTICAL output, because the
  kernel's f16 accumulators truncate every weight to a half anyway.
- **But that buffer is bound by five shaders** - the normalisation, the
  projection, the pair bias, the output projection, and the multimer's global
  attention - so narrowing it is a five-shader change with a silent failure
  mode. Which is exactly why it has not been done, and the note in
  src/evoformer/attention.js says so.

## Double-buffering the staging: 1.20x, bit-exact, and it costs nothing

The staged GEMM's loop was barrier, read global, barrier, multiply - so each K
panel exposes a full global-memory latency, covered only by whatever other
workgroups the SM happens to hold. Issuing the NEXT panel's reads before this
panel's multiplies costs `BM * BK / threads` registers - four halves a lane at
64x128x16 with 256 threads, one vec4 - and covers that latency with work the
workgroup already has.

`probe-staged-gemm-parts.js --prefetch=1` at `ef2wide`'s shape:

| | staged weights | direct weights |
|---|---:|---:|
| single-buffered | 4.579 ms | 3.190 |
| **double-buffered** | **3.198** | **2.400** |
| | 1.43x | 1.33x |

**2.400 ms is 39.3 TFLOP/s**, against 20.6 where this section started. And the
left column is the one that matters: prefetching needs neither f16 weights nor
a narrower accumulator, so unlike the other two it is available everywhere.

🔴 **THE FIRST VERSION WAS SLOWER THAN NOT PREFETCHING AT ALL, AND IT IS THIS
FILE'S OWN TRAP.** The held panel was `array<vec4<f16>, n>` indexed by
`i / threads`; that is a DYNAMIC index into an array, so WGSL puts it in
addressable memory instead of registers - exactly what `unpack2x16float(w)[at &
1u]` did to `opm.project-output`. It measured 3.190 -> **4.202** ms. Unrolled
into named variables (`src_r0`, `src_r1`, ...) with the loop turned into
straight-line blocks, it is 2.400. The rule is already in CLAUDE.md and it cost
a measurement anyway.

In the three models, all with nothing else turned on:

| | before | after | |
|---|---:|---:|---|
| ESMFold2 trunk, 300 tokens, GPU | 332.3 ms | **276.0** | 1.20x |
| OpenDDE trunk, 256 tokens, GPU | 2122.4 | **1876.3** | 1.13x |
| AF2, 400 residues, warm | 4.418 s | **4.120** | 1.07x |

🔴 **AND IT IS BIT-EXACT, WHICH IS WHY IT IS ON BY DEFAULT** where the other two
staged-matrix knobs are not. `check-staged-matrix.js` gained the arm and reads
relRMS **0** across all 384 cases; `check-transition-split.js` reads 6.01e-4
either way and `check-triangle-project-matrix.js` 2.00e-4 either way, which
matters because the gated source is staged INSIDE the prefetch and
check-staged-matrix has no `sourceGate`; and `fold-af2.js` returns atom checksum
-1817291 with the knob on and off. All four fold gates pass: AF2 pLDDT 57.28,
ESMFold2 CA-CA 3.8052, OpenDDE RMSD 1.655 / TM 0.8845, AF3 pLDDT 85.83.

### Where the three staged-matrix knobs leave each model

    ESMFold2 trunk, 300 tokens, GPU        332.3 ms
    + prefetch (default, bit-exact)        276.0    1.20x
    + f16 weights and the direct read      252.5    1.32x
    + f16 accumulators                     235.6    1.41x

    OpenDDE trunk, 256 tokens              2122.4 ms
    + prefetch                             1876.3   1.13x

    AF2, 400 residues, warm                 4.418 s
    + prefetch                              4.120   1.07x

Against where the ESMFold2 trunk started this campaign, 743.8 ms, the shipped
default is now **2.69x** and the two opt-in knobs take it to 3.16x.

## The two width rules moved, because the GEMM they were measured against did

`TRANSITION_SPLIT_MIN_CHANNELS` and `TRIANGLE_PROJECT_MATRIX_MIN_CHANNELS` are
both 192, and the measurement behind them is in this file: at AF3's 128 pair
channels the split transition was 1.8% of a trunk for 72 MiB. That was taken
against a staged matrix kernel running at **20.6 TFLOP/s**. It runs at 39.3 now,
so the width at which the matrix path starts to pay has moved - and nothing in
the repository could re-measure it, because both rules were constants. They are
`pairTransitionSplitMinChannels` and `triangleProjectMatrixMinChannels` now.

AF3 trunk, 256 tokens, `bench-trunk.js --profile --model=/model-af3-int5`:

| | GPU | warm pass | peak |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| 192 / 192, as shipped | 529.6 ms | 1040 ms | 545.3 MiB |
| the split transition at 128 | 493.6 | 987 | |
| the triangle projection at 128 | 500.6 | 996 | |
| **both at 128** | **463.1** | **966** | 608.8 |

**1.14x of GPU time and 1.08x of the pass**, for 63.5 MiB - which is the trade
the 192 was making, and it is why this lands as an ampere PRIOR rather than as
a new constant. A device without the room still declines it.

🔴 **AND IT IS MORE ACCURATE, WHICH IS THE OPPOSITE OF WHAT A WIDTH RULE USUALLY
TRADES.** `check-af3-block-any.js --model=/model-af3-int5/manifest.json`:

| | pair | single |
|---|---:|---:|
| the vector kernels, as shipped | **1.15e-1** | 1.47e-3 |
| the matrix ones | **3.21e-2** | 2.42e-4 |
| bound | 1.5e-1 | |

3.6x closer, because the staged path accumulates in **f32** where the vector
triangle accumulates in f16 - which this file already recorded as the reason the
matrix kernel is not a precision trade, and had not connected to the width rule.
AF3's shipped default was within 24% of failing its own differential gate.

The fold does not move: pLDDT 85.8285 -> 85.8313, CA-CA median 3.82407 ->
3.82413.

## Grid attention's projection on the units: OpenDDE's biggest pass, halved

After the width rules moved, `grid.project` is the largest vector kernel left in
both AF3 trunks - 58.5 ms of AF3's 464 at 256 tokens and **385.9 of OpenDDE's
1877, 21%, ahead of `grid.attend`**. It is `normalized @ W` at
`n^2 x channels -> 4 * heads * dimension`, and the vector kernel runs it at
about 16 TFLOP/s where the staged GEMM now reaches 39.

🔴 **AND ITS WEIGHTS ARE ALREADY IN THE LAYOUT THE MATRIX PATH WANTS.**
`packGridAttentionWeights` lays q, k, v and the gate out as `(channels, out, 4)`
- the four roles of one output channel in four ADJACENT columns - because the
vector kernel wanted one vec4 read a cell. That is exactly what `outputGroup`
reads. AF3's triangle projection needed its four matrices re-interleaved to
reach the units; this one arrives already there, so it is a shader rather than
a layout migration.

Two things the shared GEMM needed for it, both one line:

- **`sourceRowIndex`.** The transposed grid attention reads
  `normalized[(row % n) * n + row / n]` and writes at `row` - a pair transpose,
  not a matrix one, so `sourceTransposed` is the wrong tool.
- **Plain grouped stores.** `outputGroup.stores` took `[value, gate]` pairs,
  which is the triangle's gated form; a bare lane index now means "write this
  lane as it is", which is q/k/v/gate.

| | before | after | |
|---|---:|---:|---|
| OpenDDE `grid.project`, 256 tokens | 385.9 ms | **192.0** | 2.01x |
| ...that trunk | 1877.1 | **1685.9** | 1.11x |
| ...its warm pass | 3816 | 3566 | 1.07x |
| AF3 `grid.project` | 58.5 | **36.0** | 1.63x |
| ...that trunk | 463.9 | **441.1** | 1.05x |

It costs no memory, and the differential does not move: AF3's block pair
3.21e-2 -> 3.09e-2 and OpenDDE's 1.23e-2 -> 1.30e-2 against a 1.5e-1 bound.
6MRR still folds to RMSD 1.655 / TM 0.8845 and AF3 to pLDDT 85.82.

🔴 **AND THE CHECKER WAS MADE TO FAIL BEFORE IT WAS BELIEVED.** The transposed
direction is the one place a wrong answer here looks exactly like a right one -
same shape, same magnitude, same distribution, only the pairing wrong. Dropping
`sourceRowIndex` from the shader takes the transposed arm to relRMS **1.37**
while the untransposed arm still reads 2.60e-4, which is the gate failing on
exactly the arm it exists for. It also runs OpenDDE's 8 heads at 384 channels,
because a kernel checked at one head count is a kernel checked at one head count.

## A selection rule written into one caller leaves the other two behind

Three stacks compile the AF3 pair track - the pairformer, the MSA stack and the
template embedder - and the two functions that decide which kernels it gets,
`splitTransitionConfig` and the grid projection's, were both written inside
`pairformer-block-webgpu.js`. So every pair-track kernel choice this repository
has ever made applied to 48 blocks of 54 and to none of the other six.

An AF3 trunk's pass counts say it plainly: `grid.project` runs **108** times -
96 pairformer, 8 MSA, 4 template - and `pair-transition` **6**, all of them
outside the pairformer. At OpenDDE's widths those six were **275.6 ms of a
1665 ms trunk, 17%**, running the fused kernel the pairformer had already
measured at 3.71x slower than the split.

Both rules live in the module that owns the kernel now
(`splitTransitionConfig` in src/af3/transition-webgpu.js,
`gridProjectMatrixConfig` in src/af3/grid-project-matrix.js), which is where
`resolveGridAttendMatrix` already was. What it moved:

| OpenDDE, 256 tokens | GPU | warm pass |
|---|---:|---:|
| as this session found it | 2122.4 ms | 3816 |
| + prefetch | 1877.1 | |
| + the matrix grid projection (pairformer only) | 1685.9 | 3566 |
| + the MSA and template stacks' share of it | 1665.3 | |
| + the MSA stack's split transition | **1366.8** | **3275** |

**1.55x on that trunk**, and `pair-transition` is no longer in its top five.
AF3 at 256 tokens is 463.9 -> 437.3 over the same steps, its peak 545.3 -> 617
MiB and OpenDDE's 1814 -> 1849.

6MRR still folds to RMSD 1.655 / TM 0.8845 with CA-CA 3.6708, and AF3's fold to
CA-CA 3.82416.

### The two checkers that could not run here, and what they said once they could

`check-af3-msa-block.js` and `check-af3-template.js` opened
`/model-af3-full-f32/manifest.json` as a CONSTANT, so on this box - which has
the published int5 bundle - they 404 rather than skip, and that is why two
stacks' kernel choices had gone ungated. Both take `--model=` now. The template
one needed only a bound that follows the bundle (2e-5 for f32, 1e-4 for int5,
which reads 2.41e-5). The MSA one needed a dialect - `msaUpdateBeforeOuterProduct`
has no default because AF3 takes the outer product off the pre-update MSA and
OpenDDE off the updated one - and then it found something.

🔴 **THE MSA STACK'S PAIR OUTPUT IS 5.40e-6 WITH THE MATRIX KERNELS OFF AND
1.75e-3 WITH THE SHIPPED PROFILE.** One block at n=24 against the int5 bundle,
bisected with `--no-prior=`:

| kept | pair relRMS |
|---|---:|
| nothing | 5.40e-6 |
| `pairTransitionSplit` at 128 channels | 1.23e-4 |
| `gridAttendMatrix` | 6.84e-4 |
| `gridProjectMatrix` | 1.43e-3 |
| the shipped ampere profile | **1.75e-3** |

All three are the same thing: a kernel that multiplies in f16 where the vector
one multiplied in f32, measured against a **float32 CPU reference**.
`gridAttendMatrix` has shipped since before this session and carried the same
cost unmeasured, because this checker could not open its bundle.

🔴 **AND ONE STACK OVER THE SIGN IS THE OTHER WAY.**
`check-af3-block-any.js` reads the PAIRFORMER's pair at 1.15e-1 with the vector
kernels and 3.21e-2 with the matrix ones - better, because there the vector
triangle accumulates in f16 and the staged path accumulates in f32. Same knobs,
opposite sign, because what each is compared against is different. Neither
number alone answers "are the matrix kernels more accurate"; both together say
they trade a float32 reference for a f16 multiply and win back an f16
accumulator, and which dominates depends on the kernel.

So `check-af3-msa-block.js` runs BOTH arms now and holds each to its own bound -
1e-4 for the vector kernels, 4e-3 for the matrix ones - because a single bound
raised to admit 1.75e-3 would stop the vector arm catching anything. The matrix
arm is also 28.5 ms against 16.0 at this size. What the whole model says is
unchanged: AF3 folds to CA-CA 3.82416 and pLDDT 85.82, 6MRR to RMSD 1.655.

## AF2's q/k/v/gate on the units, without repacking its weights

It was the largest thing left in an evoformer block on the vector path: at 400
residues and 512 sequences the two projections are **12.75 ms of a 72.31 ms
block**, fused vector kernels at about 16.9 TFLOP/s. As one packed GEMM of
`rows x channels -> 4 * channels`, `probe-staged-gemm-parts.js` prices the same
work at 3.659 ms against 6.364.

Three things the shared GEMM needed, and each is a general capability rather
than a special case:

- **`weightIndex`.** `packAttentionWeights` lays the four matrices out
  SEPARATELY and contiguously - `[role][k][out]`, one stride apart - where
  `outputGroup` wants the four roles of a channel adjacent. AF3's triangle
  projection solved the same mismatch by re-laying its weights at pack time;
  this buffer is bound by FIVE shaders (the normalisation, the projection, the
  pair bias, the output projection and the multimer's global attention), so
  moving it is a five-shader change with a silent failure mode. An index
  expression does the interleave arithmetically and the pack stays as it is.
- **Expression stores.** The four lanes are the query TIMES 1/sqrt(head_dim),
  the key and value plain, and the gate through a logistic. `outputGroup.stores`
  took a lane index or a `[value, gate]` pair; a string is WGSL in
  `v0..v{size-1}`.
- **`laneBias`.** The gate carries the only bias of the four. The generic
  `bias` adds `weights[bias_offset + column]` to every lane of the group, which
  here would put the gate's bias on the query.

🔴 **AND A FOURTH, BECAUSE THE TARGET IS PACKED.** The matrix flash attention
reads q, k, v and the gate as two channels to a word, so every other caller's
rule - "this epilogue owns one channel of one row, so it cannot write half a
word" - would have forced either the vector kernel or f32 targets, and f32
targets are 210 MiB more at this shape plus twice the reads for the kernel
after. The group is EIGHT columns instead: two output channels of all four
roles, so the lane that writes the word owns both halves. `outputGroup.pack`
takes each store as a `[low, high]` pair and binds u32.

| | ms |
|---|---:|
| `msa-row-attention.project` | 6.38 -> **4.17** |
| `msa-column-attention.project` | 6.37 -> **4.16** |
| a block | 72.31 -> **67.29** |
| the stack | 3471 -> **3230** |
| a warm fold, 400 residues, 512/1024 | 4.123 -> **3.880 s** |

### ...and the output projection, which is the plainest GEMM in the model

The same operation one kernel later: `weighted @ outputWeight + outputBias`,
added into the block's activation. No interleave, no group, no gate - and one
thing a plain projection lacks, which is why it could never be the transition's
linear kernel: **the column attention writes its result back TRANSPOSED**,
`q * batch + b` where the input row is `b * queries + q`. That is `outputIndex`,
with `queries` and `batch` in MatmulParameters' two spare words.

    msa-row-attention.output      2.673 -> out of the top ten
    msa-column-attention.output   2.682 -> ...
    a block                       67.29 -> 63.56 ms
    the stack                      3230 -> 3051
    a warm fold at 400 residues   4.127 -> 3.687 s

🔴 **AND THE TRANSPOSE IS WHERE THE CHECKER HAD TO BE MADE TO FAIL**, because
both arms apply it: if BOTH ignored it they would agree, and the checker would
read 2.55e-4 while computing the wrong thing. Forcing the matrix arm's
`outputIndex` to the untransposed form takes the transposed cases to relRMS
**1.38** and leaves the untransposed ones at 2.55e-4 - two of four arms, which
is the two that exist to catch it.

**Where AF2 stands after this session**, 400 residues, 512/1024:

    a block      78.01 -> 63.56 ms    1.23x
    the stack     3745 -> 3051        1.23x
    a warm fold  4.418 -> 3.687 s     1.20x

and what is left in a block is 29% flash attention - already on the units and
already swept - 13% the two projections that just moved there, and 11% the outer
product mean.

**1.53x on the kernel.** pLDDT 57.28 -> 57.29 and the first alpha carbon moves
0.13 A - the f16 multiply the units do, and the same order as every other
kernel here that made that trade. `check-attention-project-matrix.js` reads
2.6e-4 unpacked and 3.7e-4 packed against the vector kernel's f32 arm, at 256,
128 and 64 channels.

## AF2's matrix flash attention has a race, and the reorder below found it

🔴 **THE AF2 HALF OF THE NEXT SECTION IS REVERTED, AND THE REASON IS WORTH MORE
THAN THE 5% WAS.** Moving the key tile's global reads ahead of the barrier is a
pure reordering - the diff of the two generated shaders is the same reads, the
same writes, the same five barriers in the same places, and the same arithmetic
- and with it AF2 returns **a different structure on every run**:

    the shipped kernel     -2791069  -2791069  -2791069     pLDDT 31.28 x3
    with the reorder       -2996289  -3047239  -2919633     31.21, 31.20, 31.21

400 residues, 512/1024, three whole processes each, and `--no-prior` is
deterministic, and so is every other knob in the profile taken one at a time:
`matrixLinear`, `opmMatrixContract`, `stagedMatrixPrefetch`,
`attentionProjectMatrix`, `triangleProjectMatrix`. Only `attentionMatrix`
varies, and only with the reorder.

A reordering that changes nothing semantically cannot MAKE a kernel wrong. It
can only change the timing - which means **that kernel already had a race and
the old ordering was hiding it**. The reorder is a 1.5% warm-fold win waiting
behind finding it, and the same change in AF3's `grid.attend` is deterministic
on both models that run it (three runs of `check-grid-attend-matrix.js`
identical, and AF3 folds to pLDDT 85.83158758209973 and 6MRR to
92.05076149864064 across runs).

🔴 **AND IT IS NOT THE BARRIER THAT DOES IT - IT IS THE UNROLLING.** Bisected by
keeping the barrier exactly where it was and only turning the staging loop
`for (var i = local; i < KEYS * HD4; i += LANES)` into its two straight-line
iterations: **`3 passes ... returned 3 different structures: -2711330, -2813328,
-2907558`**. Same barrier, same reads, same writes, same order; two copies
instead of a loop. So whatever is wrong is exposed by the compiler being free to
put both global loads in flight at once, which a loop with a runtime trip count
does not let it do - and the shipped kernel is deterministic only because that
loop serialises them. Restored, and the shipped form reads -2940090 three times.

Where a fix would start: the loads are of `key` and `value`, which nothing in
this dispatch writes, so hoisting them cannot be wrong on its own. Either the
extra registers change occupancy enough to expose a race elsewhere in the block
- which the knob-at-a-time bisection argues against, since only
`attentionMatrix` varies - or one of this kernel's five `workgroupBarrier()`s is
not the barrier it needs to be.

🔴 **AND `bench-af2-warm.js` IS THE DETECTOR NOW, WHICH IT ALREADY ALMOST WAS.**
It ran three predictions of the same graph over the same inputs in one process
and reported the LAST one's atoms - throwing away the comparison it was already
paying for. One process is the strong form of the test: the pipelines, the
buffers and the clocks are identical and only the SCHEDULING differs, so a
difference is a race and not a precision question. It checksums every pass now
and throws, naming all three, unless `--allow-nondeterminism` says otherwise.
Falsified against the reordered kernel: `3 passes ... returned 3 different
structures: -2678729, -2642572, -3144562`.

## The same reorder in the two flash attentions, for free

Both matrix flash kernels had the staged GEMM's shape: `barrier; read global;
write workgroup; barrier; compute`, so every key tile exposes a full memory
latency with nothing of the workgroup's own to cover it. Reading into REGISTERS
first and moving the barrier BETWEEN the read and the write puts that latency
underneath the tail of the previous tile's compute. It is a pure reordering -
the same reads, the same writes, the same arithmetic - and it needs no loop
restructuring at all, because the barrier that has to move is already inside
the tile body.

| | before | after | |
|---|---:|---:|---|
| `msa-row-attention.flash` (AF2) | 7.407 ms | 7.024 | 5.2%, **reverted** |
| `msa-column-attention.flash` | 5.465 | 5.216 | 4.6%, reverted |
| `triangle-attention-ending.flash` | 2.943 | 2.800 | 4.9%, reverted |
| AF2's block | 63.56 | 62.73 | reverted - see above |
| `grid.attend` (AF3, 256 tokens) | 77.5 | **71.7** | 7.5% |
| ...that trunk | 437.3 | **430.9** | |
| `grid.attend` (OpenDDE) | 166.3 | **154.9** | 6.9% |
| ...that trunk | 1366.8 | **1353.6** | |

Less than the 1.43x the GEMM got, and the reason is in this file already: these
kernels are OCCUPANCY-STARVED where the GEMM is not, so the SM has fewer other
workgroups whose work could have covered the latency - but it also has fewer of
them to lose to the extra registers, which is why this is free here. Sixteen
registers a lane at the shipped 4x32 tile: two pairs of vec4.

🔴 **AND THE HELD TILE IS UNROLLED INTO NAMED VARIABLES IN BOTH**, which is the
trap this file has now recorded three times: a dynamic index into a WGSL array
is addressable memory and not registers, and prefetching into one measured
SLOWER than not prefetching at all.
