## AF2's triangle multiplication, on the kernels AF3 already had

`src/triangle/` is ONE module: the projection, the contraction and the gated
output projection that AF3's pair track moved onto the units earlier in this
file are the same shaders AF2's evoformer block runs, at AF2's own widths. What
had to grow was the caller - an interleaved weight pack, four uniforms, and a
buffer for the output projection's gate - and the operation was **9.5 ms of a
62.73 ms block**, all three of its kernels vector.

    a block      62.73 -> 59.63 ms
    the stack     3011 -> 2862
    a warm fold  3.649 -> 3.498 s

The three kernels leave the block's top eight. `check-triangle-residual.js`
reads 1.08e-7 with nothing untouched, and a fold is pLDDT 57.217 -> 57.213.

🔴 **AND THE SELECTION RULE MOVED INTO THE MODULE FOR THE THIRD TIME THIS
SESSION.** `projectMatrixConfig` was a private function in
`pairformer-block-webgpu.js`; three callers ask it now - that pairformer, AF3's
MSA stack and AF2's block - so it is `triangleProjectMatrixConfig` in
src/triangle/project-matrix.js, beside `splitTransitionConfig` and
`gridProjectMatrixConfig` which moved for the same reason. The contraction's
accumulator moved with it: `createTriangleContractMatrixShader` reads
`contractResult` off its own geometry rather than making each caller remember
that its K is the protein's length.

🔴 **THE MULTIMER IS NOW BEHIND, AND DELIBERATELY.**
`src/multimer/block.js` has its own `encodeTriangleMultiplication` - the same
shaders, a second dispatch - and it keeps the vector path. That is exactly the
divergence docs/AF2.md records in the other direction, and it is left here
because **there is no multimer bundle on this box to gate the change with**. The
edit is the same one; whoever has the weights should make it and run
`check-multimer-template.js` and a multimer fold beside it.

**Where AF2 stands after this session**, 400 residues, 512/1024:

    a block      78.01 -> 59.63 ms    1.31x
    the stack     3745 -> 2862        1.31x
    a warm fold  4.418 -> 3.498 s     1.26x

## The ESMFold2 trunk was repacking its weights on every pass: wall 1.40x

At 300 tokens that trunk is 275.7 ms of GPU in **701.1 ms of wall**. The gap is
not the GPU and it is not the bus:

    packPairTrackWeights, 24 blocks, 256 channels, blocked layout      135.2 ms
    ...the INTERLEAVED layout the matrix projection reads              232.4 ms

That ran on every pass of every recycle, over weights that never change. The
interleave costs 1.7x the blocked pack because `af3TriangleWeights` splits two
fused tensors and transposes two more before `packTriangleWeights` transposes
and interleaves four projection matrices on top - all element by element, in
JavaScript.

`residentWeightBuffer` has existed for a long time and AF3's pairformer has
used it for as long; this trunk did not.

    GPU     275.7 -> 275.8 ms      unchanged, as it must be
    wall    701.1 -> 501.3         **1.40x**
    a 40-token fold end to end   9.33 -> 8.04 s

The fold is bit-for-bit what it was: CA-CA median 3.8052 and worst 3.8464,
certainty 0.7924, contact recall 0.6279, all to every digit reported.

🔴 **AND THE FILE SAID IT HAD DECLINED RESIDENCY DELIBERATELY.** The note read:
"the whole trunk is 37.9M parameters - 144 MiB in f32, against the 567 MiB an
AF3 trunk keeps resident for its single track alone - so the upload is small and
the block loop is short." That is true of the UPLOAD and says nothing about the
PACK. It is a hypothesis written in the same voice as a measurement and then
cited, which is the exact failure this document records about itself two
sections up, over `createBuffer`. The note is now the measurement.

### ...and the same thing in AF3's other two stacks

The pairformer has kept its weights resident for a long time; the MSA stack and
the template embedder never did, and they run the same pair track. The template
is worse than a per-pass repack: its blocks are re-encoded once per template
SLOT as well as once per pass, so a four-template job packed and wrote the same
weights four times over.

| warm pass, 256 tokens | AF3 | OpenDDE |
|---|---:|---:|
| before | 928 ms | 3264 |
| + the MSA stack resident | 905 | 2981 |
| + the template embedder | **883** | **2957** |
| ...its `msa-stack` stage | 182 -> **136** | -> 473 |
| ...its `template` stage | 103 -> **90** | 263 -> **241** |
| peak | 617 -> 627 MiB | 1849 -> 1915 |

GPU time does not move - 431 either way - which is what says this is host work
and nothing else. Every gate is bit-identical: AF3 folds to pLDDT
85.83158758209973 and CA-CA 3.824156513746736, 6MRR to RMSD 1.655 / TM 0.8845,
and `check-af3-msa-block.js` reads 5.40e-6 and 1.75e-3 on its two arms exactly
as before - at 9.3 ms against 27.9.

🔴 **THE CACHE KEY IS THE BLOCK OBJECT AND THE LABEL DROPS THE INDEX.** The
template used to name its buffers `w.tri.out.0`, `w.tri.out.1` and so on;
residency is keyed on the weight object, so two blocks cannot share a buffer
because they are not the same object - and a device-memory breakdown then reads
one row per tensor instead of one per block, which is what
`residentWeightBuffer`'s `variant` note asks for.

## AF2 keeps its weights too, and the note that said not to was an M2's

`src/runtime/execution.js` carried this, in the same voice as a measurement:

> WEIGHTS ARE NOT CACHED ACROSS PASSES, and that was measured, not assumed. ...
> It bought NOTHING. A 59-residue four-pass fold measured 4.3 s with the cache
> and 4.3 s without it. The fold is GPU-bound and the uploads were already
> overlapping with compute - and this is Apple Silicon, where host and device
> share memory, so writeBuffer is close to free. ... **Worth revisiting on a
> discrete GPU, where the same traffic crosses PCIe.**

This is that discrete GPU, and the answer is different - but not for the reason
the note expected. It is not the bus, it is the PACK: packing an evoformer
stack's weights is **136.6 ms of host time a pass** at 400 residues (four
attentions, two transitions and two triangles a block, 48 blocks), and the
interleaved triangle layout the matrix projection now reads is 2.6x the blocked
one on its own.

    a warm fold, 400 residues, 512/1024    3.483 -> 3.435 s
    cold                                    4.53 -> 4.16
    peak, a 59-residue fold                108.5 -> 386.9 MiB

The atom checksum does not move - `-1846490` either way, the baseline at the
time; it is `-1287025` now that fold-af2.js's synthetic alignment has
distinct rows, still identical across the arm - which is what says
this is host work and nothing else.

🔴 **AND `uploadResident` RETURNS THE OFFSETS, WHICH IS WHY A RESIDENT PACK IS
NOT A RESIDENT UPLOAD.** Every packer here returns `{data, offsets}` and the
caller needs the offsets on EVERY pass, for its uniform and for its shader - so
caching only the buffer would still run the pack to get them, which is the work
that costs the time. The offsets are a handful of integers and are kept; the
array of weights is not.

🔴 **A DEVICE WITH A MEMORY CEILING DECLINES RESIDENCY OUTRIGHT, AND FINDING OUT
WHY TOOK MAKING IT FAIL.** AF3's pairformer catches `GpuMemoryBudgetError` and
RESTARTS its stack without residency; AF2's stack has no restart. Under
`--budget=200` with residency on, the allocator instead evicts pooled buffers to
make room for the 280 MiB of resident weights - and evicts one an in-flight
command buffer still names:

    [Buffer "msa-row-attention.value"] used in submit while destroyed
    [Buffer "msa-row-attention.pair-bias"] used in submit while destroyed

**That is an allocator bug and it is not new**; residency only made the pressure
that reveals it. Recorded here rather than fixed: an eviction path that can free
a buffer a submitted command buffer references is wrong whatever asks for the
memory. Until it is fixed, `uploadResident` takes residency only when the device
has no budget at all, and `fold-af2.js --budget=` and `--no-resident` are the
two arms that say so - 108.5 MiB and 386.9, the same fold either way.

### ...and grid attention's output projection, which needed one new hook

`(gathered * logistic(gate)) @ outputProjection`, added into the pair - 128.5 ms
of an OpenDDE trunk (9.5%) and 22.4 of AF3's, at about 16 and 10 TFLOP/s.

The gate is the only thing that makes it not a plain projection, and the shared
GEMM already had the WRONG shape of gate: `sourceGate` is the SwiGLU's, two
halves of one widened row a fixed distance apart. This one lives in its own
buffer at the SOURCE's own index. `sourceModulate` is that - one extra read in a
loop that already runs, where the alternative is an elementwise pass over
`rows x width`. The transposed direction writes back UNtransposed so the
residual lands on the pair the right way round, which is `outputIndex` again.

    OpenDDE trunk, 256 tokens    1355.0 -> 1281.6 ms    warm 2957 -> 2893
    AF3 trunk                     432.0 ->  428.8       warm  883 ->  909

`grid.project-out` leaves the top five of both. The block differential does not
move - AF3 pair 3.21e-2 -> 3.08e-2, OpenDDE 1.29e-2 - and neither do the folds:
AF3 pLDDT 85.830 with CA-CA 3.82410, 6MRR RMSD 1.655 / TM 0.8845.

## The pair track's f16 weights, re-measured - and the bug the flag found

`pairWeightPrecision` defaults to f32 on a measurement that is now stale in the
same way the width rules were: it was taken when **every** kernel reading those
weights was a vector one. Most of them are staged matrix GEMMs now, and a matrix
GEMM rounds its weights to halves on the way in whatever the buffer holds - so
for those the narrower buffer is the same arithmetic. `bench-trunk.js
--pair-weights=` and `check-af3-block-any.js --pair-weights=` are what ask.

| | GPU | warm | peak | block pair |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| OpenDDE, f32 | 1284.9 ms | 2853 | 1915 MiB | 1.29e-2 |
| ...f16 | **1262.8** | 2831 | **1590** | 1.84e-2 |
| AF3, f32 | 429.6 | 892 | 627 | 3.08e-2 |
| ...f16 | 426.2 | 925 | **591** | 4.91e-2 |

**1.7% of GPU and 325 MiB**, for 1.4x on the block's residue where the note that
set the default measured 3x on the same checker and a factor of 77 on the
confidence head's pLDDT. The block's share of that cost really has shrunk. The
CONFIDENCE head is the number that decided it and no checker here can run it, so
the default does not move - but the memory is worth someone's measurement on a
box that has the full bundle.

🔴 **AND THE DIRECT WEIGHT READ IS A LOSS AT THESE SHAPES, WHICH IS WORTH
KNOWING.** With f16 pair weights it is available for the first time on this
track, and OpenDDE's trunk goes 1262.8 -> 1275.1 and AF3's 426.2 -> 434.6. On
ESMFold2 the same option is 1.12x. The difference is N: these kernels have 1536
and 512 columns against the transition's 2048, so a subgroup's 16-column strip
of a direct read is a narrower burst.

🔴 **AND THE FLAG FOUND A BUG THAT NOTHING ELSE COULD HAVE.**
`packGridAttentionWeights` takes a shape and nothing else and always writes a
`Float32Array`, where `packTriangleWeights` and `packTransitionWeights` both
take the element. Wiring the new grid matrix kernels to `weightPrecision` -
which is right for every other kernel in that file - told the shader the buffer
held halves when it holds floats, and `check-af3-block-any.js` returned **NaN**.
The shipped default is f32, so nothing was ever wrong on the shipped path; the
fault would have been waiting for whoever narrowed the pair weights next. The
grid shaders name `"f32"` explicitly now, with the reason beside them.

## Where the four models stand, end to end

Everything above, measured on the shipped defaults at the end of the session
that produced it. `before` is this repository at commit `04799bb`, which is
after the earlier A100 campaign and before any of the work in the sections
above.

| | before | after | |
|---|---:|---:|---|
| **AF2**, 400 residues, 512/1024, warm fold | 4.418 s | **3.437** | 1.29x |
| ...a block | 78.01 ms | **59.63** | 1.31x |
| ...cold | 4.82 | 4.89 | one more pipeline |
| **AF3**, 256 tokens, warm trunk pass | 1026 ms | **886** | 1.16x |
| ...its GPU | 529.6 | **429.7** | 1.23x |
| **OpenDDE**, 256 tokens, warm trunk pass | 3816 ms | **2843** | 1.34x |
| ...its GPU | 2122.4 | **1284.9** | 1.65x |
| **ESMFold2**, 300 tokens, trunk GPU | 332.3 | **275.9** | 1.20x |
| ...its wall | 701.1 | **502.9** | 1.39x |
| ...a 40-token fold | 9.33 s | **8.04** | 1.16x |
| ...a 91-token fold, and every one after the first | 8.26 s | **1.03** | **8.0x** |

🔴 **AND THE LAST ROW IS THE ONE A USER SEES.** A page folds more than once, and
until this session every model in it re-decoded and re-uploaded its weights on
every fold. The measurements above are all first folds except that one.
`fold.js --model=/model-af3-int5 --folds=3` is 3.741, 1.234, 1.145 s.

What did it, roughly in order of what each was worth:

1. **Double-buffering the staged GEMM's operand reads** (bit-exact, default on):
   1.20x on ESMFold2's trunk, 1.13x OpenDDE, 1.07x AF2.
2. **Four more kernels onto the matrix units** - grid attention's projection and
   output projection, AF2's q/k/v/gate and output projections, AF2's triangle
   multiplication - each 1.5x to 2x on its own kernel.
3. **The two width rules re-measured**, which put AF3's pair track on the matrix
   path at all: 1.14x of its GPU, and MORE accurate.
4. **A selection rule that lived in one caller of three**, which had left the
   MSA stack and the template embedder on the vector kernels whatever the
   profile said: 1.13x of an OpenDDE trunk on its own.
5. **Weights packed once instead of once per pass**, in five stacks that were
   all doing it: ESMFold2's trunk wall 701 -> 501, AF3's MSA stack and template
   embedder 928 -> 883, AF2's 3.48 -> 3.44 with a much larger cold win, and the
   ESM-C tower, which is the largest of all of them - 8.26 s to 1.85 on every
   fold after the first.

And two knobs that are measured, checked, and OFF, because deciding them needs
an oracle this box cannot run: `stagedMatrixDirectWeights` (bit-exact, needs an
f16 weight buffer) and `stagedMatrixResult: "f16"` (1.19x, and it must never
reach a kernel whose K is the input's size).

Three things this session found that are not speedups:

- **AF2's matrix flash attention has a race.** A reordering that changes nothing
  semantically makes the fold non-reproducible. `bench-af2-warm.js` is the
  detector now.
- **The allocator can free a buffer an in-flight submit still names**, under
  memory pressure. Residency made the pressure; the eviction is the bug.
- **`packGridAttentionWeights` has no precision parameter** where its two
  siblings do, which is a silent trap for the next caller that narrows the pair
  weights.

## The pairformer's wall is not scheduling, and a fence nobody read

An OpenDDE trunk pass is 2805 ms with the pairformer 1702 of it, against a
labelled GPU total of 1283 for the WHOLE pass - which looks like the stack
spending most of its time waiting for nothing. It is not:

    submission window     1      4     16     48
    the pairformer      1791   1723   1707   1713 ms
    ...of which wait    1534   1468   1456   1444

**Flat past four.** Draining the queue every block costs 5%; everything wider
than that is the same number, so the GPU is not idling between submissions and
there is no scheduling win here. The stack's own accounting says the same from
the other side: the host encodes 48 blocks in 16 ms.

🔴 **ONE THING WAS FREE, AND IT WAS A FENCE NOBODY READ.** The loop took
`queue.onSubmittedWorkDone()` after every block to drive `onBlockDone` - 48
fences a pass - whether or not a caller had passed that callback. The page
passes it; every bench and every checker does not. Asking the driver for a
signal nothing reads is worth **1577 -> 1487 ms** of the pairformer's wait.

🔴 **AND THE PROFILER IS NOT THE EXPLANATION EITHER**, which is worth writing
down because it is the obvious suspicion. `--profile` gives every dispatch its
own timestamped pass rather than batching them; measured against no profiler at
all, OpenDDE's pass is 2852 with and 2805 without and AF3's 886 against 849.
Two to four percent, not the gap.

## Where an AF3 fold's non-compute time is, on this card

`fold.js --buffers` at 68 tokens and 50 steps, 3745 ms of wall:

    buffer traffic          941 ms   25%
    queue drain             927 ms   25%  over 178 onSubmittedWorkDone (a union)
      mapAsync              468 ms  12.5%  x86      54.2 MiB
      writeBuffer           330 ms   8.8%  x1723  2251.1 MiB
      createBindGroup        75 ms   2.0%  x4643
      computePass(encode)    34 ms   0.9%  x17483
      createBuffer           16 ms   0.4%  x2567  2606.0 MiB
      queue.submit            9 ms   0.2%  x635

and by label, the rows worth a name:

    353 ms x50      0.9 MiB   mapAsync dec.rb
    100 ms x480   228.0 MiB   writeBuffer (unlabelled)
     77 ms x1      81.2 MiB   writeBuffer difftx.zerogate.resident
     75 ms x4643             createBindGroup (unlabelled)
     28 ms x50     56.3 MiB   writeBuffer np.projection
     21 ms x56     42.1 MiB   writeBuffer w.pair-transition
     18 ms x116    35.6 MiB   writeBuffer w.grid

🔴 **THE SINGLE LARGEST ROW IS FIFTY READBACKS OF EIGHTEEN KILOBYTES.**
`dec.rb` is the atom decoder's position update, mapped to the host once per
diffusion step - 0.9 MiB in total and **353 ms, 9.4% of the fold**, which is
latency and nothing else: a `mapAsync` drains the queue, and the sampler's next
step cannot start until it returns. `createBuffer` is 16 ms for 2.6 GiB, so
allocation is still free; `writeBuffer` is 330 ms for 2.25 GiB, which is the bus
doing bus work.

The residency added in the sections above shows here as small one-time rows -
`w.pair-transition` 21 ms over 56 buffers, `w.grid` 18 over 116, `w.tri.out` and
`w.tri.in` 12 and 11 - where before they were per pass.

What that leaves as the next thing worth doing to AF3, and it is not a kernel:
**the sampler's step is a host round trip by construction.** The denoiser writes
the update, the host maps it, does the EDM step in JavaScript, and writes the
next input back. Keeping that state on the device would take the 353 ms and most
of the 100 ms of unlabelled per-step writes with it. It is an architecture
change, it is out of scope for a kernel session, and the number is now measured
rather than guessed at.

## The GPU dequantiser was int5-at-32, and ESM-C is int3-at-128

**71% of an ESMFold2 fold is the language model.** At 40 tokens the fold is 8.16
s and `language model` is 5816 ms of it; the trunk, after everything above, is
705 (and its recycles 2, 3 and 4 are 33, 37 and 32 ms each). The tower is not
compute-bound - `bench-esmc-tower.js` puts a whole 36-block forward at 1.16 s at
61 tokens and 1.33 at 300, which is 0.27 TFLOP/s - it is bound on getting its
weights to the device. src/esmc/tower-webgpu.js says how: it STREAMS them, one
block ahead, decoding int3 and narrowing to f16 on the host at **1121 ms and 723
ms across 36 blocks, against 660 ms of GPU**.

`src/runtime/quantised-upload.js` exists to do exactly that decode on the GPU
instead, and AF3's diffusion transformer has used it for a long time. It could
not help ESM-C, because it was int5 at a group of 32 written into its constants
(`GROUP = 32`, `GROUP_BYTES = 20`) and into its shader (`* 5u`, `& 31u`). ESM-C
is the only bundle here that is not int5: **int3 at a group of 128**.

Both are the same arithmetic at a different width, and a codec is legal when a
group is a whole number of bytes and a code spans at most two of them - which
holds for any width up to eight. The decoder takes `(bits, group)` off the
tensor's own record now, and the pipeline cache is keyed on it.

🔴 **AND THE CHECKER FOR IT DRIVES THE SHIPPED PLANNER, WHICH `check-int5-gpu.js`
DOES NOT.** That file re-implements the decode in its own WGSL and compares that
against the host, which checks the ARITHMETIC and not the kernel - the offsets,
the group spans, the dynamic uniform stride and the two-element word are where
an off-by-one lives and none of them exist in a comparison shader.
`check-quantised-upload.js` runs `planBlockUpload` and `runBlockUpload`, with
two tensors so `firstElement`, `codeBase`, `groupBase` and `destWord` all have
to agree, and holds them to **zero differing elements** across six codecs.

🔴 **AND IT FOUND ONE IMMEDIATELY, ON THE HOST SIDE.** At int5 group 64 the two
decoders disagreed on **104,170 of 131,072 elements** - and it is
`readTensorRange` that is wrong: its int5 fast path reads a 20-byte group
because 32 x 5 is 160 bits, and strides by it whatever the record says. The
generic branch beside it handles any (bits, group); five bits at 32 skips it
only for an unrolled loop worth 3.8 s on a bundle. It throws now rather than
returning a finite, plausible, wrong tensor.

## The denoiser kept its weights too: the second fold is 8.3 s -> 1.03

With the tower's weights resident, a second ESMFold2 fold is 1.84 s and
**`conditioning` is 891 ms of it - 48%**. `Esmfold2DenoiserGpu.prepare` runs
once per fold and pushed every weight it has through the pooled allocator,
narrowing the token blocks to f16 on the way. Keyed on the weight objects
instead - `block.attention` and `block.transition` are distinct per block, so
two blocks cannot share a buffer - and with the value a thunk so a hit never
runs the narrowing:

    conditioning     891 -> 96 ms
    the second fold  1.84 -> 1.03 s
    the first fold   8.26 -> 8.27      unchanged, as it must be

**8x on every fold after the first**, over where this session found it, and the
structure is bit-identical at every repeat.

🔴 **AND THE ALLOCATOR'S PEAK STOPPED BEING THE DEVICE'S.** Resident buffers are
created outside the pool - they have to outlive the run that made them - so
`allocator.snapshot()` no longer sees the largest thing a fold holds: it read
344 MiB before this work and **94 after**, while the device held 1583.
`fold-esmfold2.js` reports both now, and `devicePeakMebibytes` is the one that
answers "will this fit". A number that got SMALLER as the fold got heavier is
exactly the kind this file exists to catch.

Sized against the budget the same way, and the two arms are different for the
two stacks, which is the point of sizing rather than refusing:

| `--budget=` | second fold | `conditioning` | device peak |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| none | 1.042 s | 98 ms | 1583.1 MiB |
| 3000 MiB | 6.130 | 95 | 488.6 |

At 3000 the TOWER declines - 890 MiB of weights needs four times that - and the
denoiser still takes it, because its own are small. Both fold identically.

## ESM-C's weights, kept: every fold after the first is 8.3 s -> 1.85

The tower STREAMS its 36 blocks - decode one out of int3, narrow it to f16,
upload, run, release - so that 2190 MiB of float32 never exists at once. That is
what makes the first fold possible on a small device, and it is what makes every
fold after it pay the whole 1.8 seconds of host work again over weights that
never change.

`fold-esmfold2.js --repeat=3` folds from the top each time, reusing nothing,
which is what a page does when a user pastes a second sequence:

    fold 1     8.26 s     language model 5612 ms
    fold 2     1.859      ...152
    fold 3     1.853      ...166

**4.5x on every fold after the first**, and the language model 37x. The
structure is bit-identical - the repeat arm compares alpha carbons against the
first fold and throws on any difference, so a residency that returned something
else could not pass as a stopwatch win.

🔴 **A HIT SKIPS THE DECODE, NOT JUST THE UPLOAD, WHICH IS WHY THIS IS NOT
`residentWeightBuffer`.** That one takes a `pack` thunk and calls it on a miss -
by which time the caller has already awaited `blockWeights(layer)`, which is the
1121 ms. This is asked BEFORE the layer's promise is created, and the ten
per-tensor values became thunks for the same reason: JavaScript evaluates an
argument before the function can decide it does not need it, and `narrow(...)`
of a 33 MiB tensor is the other 723 ms.

🔴 **AND A CEILING IS ANSWERED BY SIZE, NOT BY REFUSING OUTRIGHT.** The rule
AF2's residency takes - "no budget, no residency" - would have given this to a
bench and to nobody else, because **the page always sets a budget**:
`web/model.js` passes `memoryBudgetBytes: null`, which means "guess one from
`navigator.deviceMemory`". What the weights cost is known here - ten tensors a
block, four of them big, in halves, about 890 MiB over 36 blocks - so the line
is a quarter of the ceiling. A 16 GiB desktop guesses about 5.5 GiB and takes
it; a 4 GiB phone guesses 1.3 and streams as before. `fold-esmfold2.js --budget=`
is what says so, and it was made to fire:

    --budget=2000   fold 2 in 6.856 s, language model 5188   streamed
    --budget=8000   fold 2 in 1.833 s, language model 149    resident

both `sameFold true`. The estimate is still only an estimate, so an allocation
that crosses the ceiling anyway raises before `createBuffer`, gives up on
residency for this device for good, and streams from that block on - partial
residency being safe because a block's buffers are independent of each other's.

## Half of an OpenDDE fold is a host implementation, and nothing was timing it

An OpenDDE fold of 6MRR is 23.5 s and its stage timings summed to **7.2**. Two
things were hiding the rest, and both are properties of the instrument rather
than of the model:

- **`onStage` attributes the gap between two notifications to the earlier one**,
  so a stage that never fires makes its work invisible - and `src/af3/fold.js`
  named nothing at all after `trunk-done`.
- **The last stage never closes itself.** Whatever ran after the final
  notification - the confidence head, the readbacks - was not counted either.

`sample-start` and `sample-done` are stages now, and `fold-opendde.js` closes
the last one before it stops the clock. The fold then accounts for itself
exactly, 20744 ms of 20766:

    structural-conditioning   10083 ms
    pairformer-block           4425
    sample-done                2517
    sample-start               1355
    trunk-stage                1319
    structural-refine           725

🔴 **AND THE SAMPLER IS NOT WHERE THE TIME IS, WHICH IS THE OPPOSITE OF WHAT A
200-STEP FOLD LOOKS LIKE.** Dropping the step count from 200 to 20 takes the
fold from 23.5 s to 20.9 - so a step is **14.5 ms** and the whole sampler is 2.9
s of it. Everything else is fixed cost.

🔴 **`structural-conditioning` IS 10.1 SECONDS AND IT IS `diffusionConditioning`
FROM diffusion-reference.js - THE HOST IMPLEMENTATION.**
`Af3DiffusionConditioningGpu` sits beside it and AF3's own diffusion head has
used it all along; only the STRUCTURAL-TOKEN branch, which is OpenDDE's, calls
the reference. At 130 structural tokens and 384 pair channels that is
`tokens^2 x channels` of layer norms, projections and transitions in JavaScript.

Swapping it in takes the stage to **430 ms** and the fold to **13.9 s** - and
the fold is then **not finite**.

🔴 **ITS CHECKER HAS BEEN PASSING ON A NaN, AND THAT IS ALL THAT IS ESTABLISHED.**
`check-af3-diffusion-conditioning.js` needed a `--model=` first, being the third
checker here pinned to a bundle this box does not have. With one, its two arms
on AF3's bundle read pair 2.66e-7 and single 7.09e-7 - the GPU conditioning
agrees with the reference there. On **OpenDDE's** bundle the same field reads
`"pair": null`, which is what `JSON.stringify` does to **NaN**:

    alphafold3   pair NaN   single 8.78e-7
    openbind0    pair NaN   single 9.39e-7

and the gate was `if (worst > bound) throw`. **`NaN > 1e-5` is false**, so a
residual that is not a number read as within bound, and this arm has been
reporting nothing at all for as long as that bundle has existed.

The checker rejects a non-finite residual by name now, its bound is
`!(worst <= bound)` rather than `worst > bound`, and it says WHICH SIDE is bad -
because "NaN" alone sends the next person to the wrong file. What it says is:

    alphafold3's pair residual is NaN, not a number.
    GPU: 73728/73728 non-finite; reference: 73728/73728 non-finite

**Both** sides, entirely. So this is the CHECKER'S OWN FIXTURE at 384 pair
channels - its synthetic input or the weights it splices - and not evidence
against either kernel. Whether the GPU pair conditioning is right at OpenDDE's
widths is still **unknown**, and the checker cannot answer it until its fixture
is finite there. AF3's bundle passes on both arms at 2.66e-7.

🔴 **AND EVERY COMPARISON WRITTEN `if (x > bound) throw` IN THIS REPOSITORY HAS
THE SAME HOLE.** That is the shape to grep for.

So the swap is reverted. The largest single win left here is still **half of an
OpenDDE fold, ten seconds**, and what stands between is now two known things
rather than one guess: the checker's fixture does not survive 384 pair channels,
and with the fixture fixed the kernel can finally be asked. Both are ahead of
touching `fold.js` again.

## The multimer, which every one of these wins had skipped

`src/multimer/block.js` and `src/evoformer/block.js` share every SHADER and
diverge in the DISPATCH - the hazard docs/AF2.md records in the other direction
- so the attention projections, the triangle multiplication and the weight
residency this session gave the monomer applied to 48 blocks of one family and
none of the other. The bundle is not on this box by default either; it is
`af2-multimer/` in the published registry, eight shards and 98 MiB, and it
needs a `manifest.json` generated from `src/reference/manifests/multimer.js`
the way AF3's does.

Wired: `attentionProjectMatrixConfig` and its output twin, the three matrix
triangle kernels, and `uploadResident` for attention, transition, outer product
mean and triangle. `fold-af2.js --family=multimer` is the gate, and it gained
`--repeat=` so residency can be priced at all.

| 59 residues, 128 rows | first fold | a repeat |
|---|---:|---:|
| before | 1128 ms | 693 / 734 |
| after | 1485 | **218 / 204** |

| 400 residues, 256/512 rows | first fold | a repeat |
|---|---:|---:|
| before | 4356 ms | 3796 |
| after | **4097** | **3290** |

**3.2x on a short repeat fold and 1.15x on a long one.** pLDDT 54.255 -> 54.234
with ptm 0.3667 -> 0.3665 and iptm 0.1043 -> 0.1042 at 59 residues; at 400,
32.642 -> 32.637 with ptm and iptm unmoved. Every repeat is held to the first
fold's atom checksum and throws on a difference.

🔴 **THE FIRST FOLD IS SLOWER, AND IT IS PIPELINE COMPILATION.** At 59 residues
the matrix kernels take a single fold from 1069 to 1439 ms, which looked like a
26% regression until it was split: `bench-af2-warm.js --length=59 --rows=128`
reads **cold 1.144 -> 1.390 and warm 0.192 -> 0.187**. The steady state is
slightly FASTER even at that size; what the matrix path costs is 246 ms of
compiling four more shaders, once per process. That is the right trade for a
page and the wrong one for a one-shot benchmark, and it is why
`--repeat=` had to exist before any of these numbers meant anything.

🔴 **AND A FIRST-IN-SESSION MEASUREMENT IS NOT A BASELINE.** The very first
multimer fold of this session read 2039 ms against the 1114 and 1084 the same
unchanged code gave immediately after - a shader cache somewhere below Chrome.
Comparing against it would have credited residency with a 2x it did not earn.

## The other half of an OpenDDE fold: the conditioning kernel that did not exist

The entry above named `structural-conditioning` as 10.1 seconds of a 20.8-second
OpenDDE fold, said the GPU swap made the fold non-finite, and concluded "the
kernel is not what is wrong". **The kernel is exactly what was wrong**, and the
reason nobody could see it is a fault this repository already has a rule about.

`Af3DiffusionConditioningGpu` had **no split-pair branch at all**. AlphaFold 3
concatenates the trunk pair (128) with the RAW relative encoding (139) and
LayerNorms the 267 together - which is what lets that shader carry the relative
encoding in closed form rather than materialising a `tokens^2 x 267` tensor.
OpenDDE LayerNorms the trunk pair on its OWN 384 channels, projects it to 128,
projects the relative encoding to 128 separately, and concatenates THOSE. It is
a different function, not a reshape: the joint LayerNorm over the widened
concatenation couples the two terms and the split one does not. The shader knew
only the first, ran anyway against a 256-row projection indexed as 267, and
`relativeColumnSums` read `scale[128 + c]` off the end of a 256-long norm and
handed it a buffer of NaN.

🔴 **AND THE CHECKER HAD `PAIR_CHANNELS = 128` TYPED INTO IT.** So pointing
`check-af3-diffusion-conditioning.js` at OpenDDE's bundle built an AF3-shaped
fixture against OpenDDE-shaped tensors: the reference read off the end of the
same projection, both sides were NaN, `NaN > bound` is false, and the arm
passed. That is CLAUDE.md's own recorded fault - *a checker that constructs its
own inputs tests whatever it constructed* - and here it hid ten seconds a fold
for as long as the port has existed.

The repairs, in the order they mattered:

- **`conditioningWeights(store, dialect)` is its own export**, lifted out of the
  object literal inside `diffusionWeights`. The checker goes through the loader
  the fold uses, so every width is the tensor's and a second bundle's widths
  arrive by themselves.
- **The split kernel is one dispatch, a workgroup per pair row.** The
  `2 * C_PAIR` concatenation is 1 KiB and lives in workgroup memory, so the
  intermediate - 92 MB at 300 tokens - never exists. A workgroup rather than a
  lane because the trunk compression alone is 384x128 multiply-accumulates
  against the joint path's 128x128, and a lane-per-row kernel re-reads the trunk
  row once per output column.
- **The bare initial-projection arm is a gate.** It was a printed number, and it
  is the one arm that isolates this kernel from the two transitions stacked on
  it.

| | before | after |
|---|---:|---:|
| `structural-conditioning` | 10,100 ms | **129 ms** |
| whole 6MRR fold | 20.8 s | **13.5 s** |
| RMSD / TM | 1.68 / 0.865 | 1.655 / 0.8845 |

`check-af3-diffusion-conditioning.js`: AF3 pair 2.7e-7, OpenDDE pair 4.0e-7 at
`split: true`, `trunkPairChannels: 384`. AF3's arm is untouched - `split` is
false there and the closed form still runs.

## "The cheap half" was 1.6 seconds: OpenDDE's PAE and PDE

`openddeConfidence`'s host half carries a comment calling itself *"the cheap
half - the per-token projections are n, not n^2"*. Two of them are not. PAE and
PDE are each `tokens^2 x 384 x 64` multiply-accumulates - **415 million apiece**
at 130 structural tokens - and inside a 6MRR fold they measured 777 ms and
821 ms.

| stage | before | after |
|---|---:|---:|
| `sample-done` | 2523 ms | **1064 ms** |
| whole 6MRR fold | 13.5 s | **12.1 s** |

A workgroup a pair row, a lane a bin: the pair row's 384 reads are a broadcast
inside the workgroup, the projection reads are coalesced across the bins, and
the softmax is two workgroup reductions. **The expectation runs in the same
dispatch**, so what comes back is one float a pair rather than 64 - 68 KiB
instead of 4.3 MB, and no host loop over the bins at all. pLDDT 92.0508, RMSD
1.655, TM 0.8845: the same three digits.

🔴 **AND THIS HEAD HAD NO DIFFERENTIAL GATE AT ALL.** Its only check was a whole
OpenDDE fold, and a fold's gate is the chain's geometry - which no confidence
number touches. So 1.6 seconds of arithmetic could be moved with nothing able to
say whether it still computed the same numbers.
`tools/gpu/check-opendde-confidence.js` is that gate: the GPU against
`hostPairReadouts`, which is the code the fold used before and is kept for
exactly this. Two token counts, one of them **odd**, because PDE reads the
TRANSPOSED row and a square fixture lets a symmetric mistake survive at one
size; and it asserts PAE and PDE are actually different, because a kernel that
ignored `symmetrise` would otherwise agree with a reference that did too.

    17 tokens  pae 2.97e-7  pde 7.82e-7  pae-vs-pde 6.15e-1
    40 tokens  pae 2.88e-7  pde 6.17e-7  pae-vs-pde 5.97e-1

Where the fold's 12.1 seconds now are, at 6MRR and 130 structural tokens:

| stage | ms |
|---|---:|
| `pairformer-block` (the trunk's 48, over the recycles) | 4431 |
| `sample-start` (200 sampler steps) | 4040 |
| `trunk-stage` | 1317 |
| `sample-done` (the confidence head's four blocks and its host gathers) | 1064 |
| `structural-refine` (four more blocks) | 727 |

## The pairformer is 96% GPU-idle, and residency is priced by the second fold

`fold-opendde.js` grew the three flags AF3's and ESMFold2's fold tools already
had - `--profile`, `--buffers`, `--repeat` - because a twelve-second fold had no
way to say where its time was. The first thing they said:

    passes 2048   sumMs 287.41   spanMs 7742.5   idleShare 0.963   submits 3882

**The GPU is idle 96.3% of the trunk phase.** Every kernel in it sums to 287 ms
of a fold that takes twelve seconds. That is the number to hold beside any
further kernel work on this model.

Where the wall went: `foldBatch` declined resident block weights for any pair
track 256 channels or wider, on a table written into the comment beside it -
873.5 MiB against 647.6, and "at most 0.4 s" across three recycles. **That table
is of one fold, which is the one fold residency cannot pay for.** Inside a
single fold the first pass packs and uploads all 48 blocks either way; what
residency buys there is only the recycles. The row a user sees is the second
fold:

|  | first fold | second fold | its `pairformer-block` |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| non-resident | 12.0 s | 7.47 s | 2890 ms |
| resident | 12.1 s | **4.46 s** | **29 ms** |

Sixty milliseconds a block, forty-eight blocks, on every fold after the first,
and the same structure to the bit (worst displacement 0.0). So the rule is the
device's room and not the width: `pairTrackResidentBytes` says what would
actually be held, and the fold keeps it when there is no budget or the budget is
at least three times that - the peak being these weights plus the pair scratch
beside them. 1798 MiB peak resident against 1112.9 non-resident;
`--budget=1500` takes the refusal and folds the same structure at 7.63 s, so the
fallback is exercised rather than assumed.

🔴 **AND SIZING THE BLOCKS BY WALKING THEM COST 2.2 SECONDS.** A block's
properties are not all plain arrays - reading one decodes it - so
`Object.values` over all 48 is 48 blocks of int5 decoded to answer a question
about their size. One block times the count, memoised: `msa-depth` went from
2197 ms back to 53.

## Where a fold's host time actually is: the quantised decode

Three fold tools now carry `--buffers`, and on ESMFold2 it said the first fold's
7990 ms is only **1088 ms** of buffer traffic. Timing the tower's block reads
found the rest: **5482 ms of 7990 is `readTensorAsFloat16`**, the int3-at-128
decode of ESM-C's four big matrices on the main thread, 573 M parameters.

The GPU dequantiser has understood that codec since it stopped being int5-at-32
only, and nothing was pointed at it.

| | before | after |
|---|---:|---:|
| ESMFold2 `language model`, first fold | 5649 ms | **978 ms** |
| ESMFold2 whole first fold | 7.99 s | **3.22 s** |
| OpenDDE first fold (pair transition too) | 12.1 s | **11.2 s** |
| ...its `pairformer-block` | 4516 ms | 3545 ms |

Both arms fold the same structure to sixteen digits - `--host-decode` on
`fold-esmfold2.js` is the arm, and it gives caSpacing mean
3.8059263862988817 and aligned error 7.484 either way; OpenDDE gives RMSD 1.655,
TM 0.8845, pLDDT 92.0508 either way.

How it is plumbed. A caller hands the tower a `sources` map beside a block's
weights - leaf name to `{record, buffer, byteOffset}`, exactly what
`HttpTensorStore.tensorSource` returns - and the four narrow leaves are decoded
straight into their device buffer. **The leaves themselves become getters**: a
value would do the decode this exists to skip. A caller offering no sources gets
the host path unchanged, which is every checker and every bench.

🔴 **AND THE SAME MOVE ON THE PAIR TRACK CHANGED NOTHING UNTIL THE PACKER WAS
TOLD.** Of the pair track's five packed tensors the transition is the only one
the device decoder can take - its packer lays four tensors end to end and
reshapes nothing, while the matrix path INTERLEAVES the triangles' four
projections and TRANSPOSES their two output matrices, and `runBlockUpload`
writes each tensor as one contiguous run. But `packPairTrackWeights` returns all
five from one call, so a caller that stopped BINDING the transition was still
paying to build it. The first measurement after the swap read 4516 ms against
4477 and looked like the transition simply not being expensive; with the packer
told to skip it, 3545.

🔴 **AND `check-quantised-upload.js` COULD NOT HAVE CAUGHT A MISTAKE HERE.** It
lays out its own shard, so it holds the kernel and the planner to a layout it
also wrote; what it cannot see is a real bundle whose records say something the
planner reads differently - a `scaleOffset` rebased against the wrong base, a
shape whose product is not the element count, a tensor starting mid-group.
`tools/gpu/check-bundle-device-decode.js` takes the biggest and the smallest
quantised tensors of an ACTUAL bundle and compares the two decoders bit for bit:
zero differing halves on all three, from 56,623,104 elements down to 1.

🔴 **AND IT FAILED FIRST ON ITSELF, IN THE DIRECTION THAT WOULD HAVE BEEN
BELIEVED.** `readTensorAsFloat16` hands back a Float16Array whose elements read
as NUMBERS; the device hands back the WORDS it wrote. Comparing one against the
other reported 7,066,042 of 7,077,888 elements differing - a checker failing,
not a decoder, and exactly the shape of mistake that passes unexamined when it
goes the other way.

🔴 **AND `check-af3-block-any.js` HAD NEVER RUN THE RESIDENT PATH.** It
hard-coded `residentWeights: false`, so the weight path a fold actually takes
was checked by no differential gate at all. `--resident` is that arm now: on
OpenDDE at f16 pair weights the two agree to every digit, pair
0.018417181287129433 either way.

## What the sampler is, measured rather than assumed

The note above ("Where an AF3 fold's non-compute time is") ends by naming the
sampler's host round trip as the next thing worth doing, at 353 ms of 3745. It
is not, and `probe-sampler-overhead.js` says so directly - 59 tokens, 12 steps:

    hostArithmetic 0.7   outsideHeadRun 0.5   insideHeadRunNotGpu 0.3
    phases: augment 0.1, inject 0.6, headRun 10.3, euler 0, wall 10.8, gpu 10

**A sampler step is 93% GPU.** The `mapAsync dec.rb` row that reads as 1962 ms
across 200 steps in a fold's buffer profile is mostly the step's own compute
being waited for, not latency. Keeping the positions on the device would buy
about 7%, for an architecture change across four files.

Two more things that were checked before concluding it:

- **The split-K sweep still stands.** `bench-head.js --splitk=` at 4/8/16/32
  gives 12/11/11/11 ms and `off` gives 16, so the Ampere prior's 16 is on the
  plateau. (The flag was rewritten first: it replaced the whole
  `diffusionSplitK` object with two of its seven fields, so it moved five knobs
  while claiming to sweep one.)
- **The profiler is not free at this scale.** A denoiser call is 11 ms without
  `--profile` and 16 with - 45%, not the 2-4% measured on a trunk pass, because
  a step is 312 short passes and each gets its own timestamped pass. Any
  per-pass gap read off a profiled sampler is inflated by that.

## The pair track's packing, on the device: OpenDDE's first fold 11.2 s -> 7.6

With the ESM-C tower's decode moved, the largest single item left in a fold was
`packPairTrackWeights`. Timed per tensor inside a 6MRR OpenDDE fold, over 56
blocks:

    tri-out 1194 ms   tri-in 1204   grid1 641   grid2 654   transition 126

**3.55 seconds of an 11.1-second first fold**, and every one of those is an int5
decode and a narrowing wrapped around a RESHAPE. That is why
`residentPackedOnDevice` could take ESM-C and not this: it wrote one contiguous
run per tensor, and a run is what a copy is, not what a transpose is.

So the decoder reshapes now. A plan entry carries `sources` (up to four) and a
stride triple; destination element d reads

    part = d % parts;  d2 = d / parts
    src  = bias[part] + (d2 / inner) * outerStride + (d2 % inner) * innerStride

The DESTINATION stays contiguous, which is the whole design: one invocation
still owns one whole word, so this is a plain store rather than an atomic
read-modify-write of sixteen bits. That covers a transpose, AF3's interleaved
a/b split, and the matrix path's four-role interleave. **The bases are selected,
not subscripted** - `params.codeBase[part]` is a dynamic vector index, which
this file's own rules say costs 4x.

Grid attention needed a second mode. Its packed buffer is float32 whatever the
pair track holds, so it decodes one element a word - and that is also the only
mode that may STRIDE the destination, because an f32 element IS a word. Its four
projections are interleaved four to a slot and cannot share one source mapping
(v is stored the way the kernel reads it; q, k and the gate are transposed into
v's layout when packed), so each is its own dispatch writing every fourth word.

| | first fold | `pairformer-block` |
|---|---:|---:|
| before | 12148 ms | 4516 ms |
| pair transition on the device | 11186 | 3545 |
| ...and the triangles | 9241 | 1532 |
| ...and grid attention | 7760 | 445 |
| ...and the MSA stack and template embedder | **7613** | 457 |

RMSD 1.655, TM 0.8845, pLDDT 92.0508 throughout - the same fold at every step.

Three things this cost, each of which looked like a result:

🔴 **THE TRANSITION SWAP READ 4516 -> 4477 AND LOOKED LIKE THE TRANSITION NOT
BEING EXPENSIVE.** `packPairTrackWeights` returns all five from one call, so a
caller that stopped BINDING a buffer was still paying to build it. It needed a
`want`.

🔴 **THE GRID SWAP READ 1532 -> 1481 AND LOOKED THE SAME WAY.** The triangle
version refuses when the planner sends a RESHAPED entry to its host list, and I
copied that rule; grid attention's two LayerNorm vectors are float32 in every
bundle, so `host` was never empty and the whole pack was refused. A reshaped
host entry is a refusal; a contiguous one is a `writeBuffer` of the tensor's own
bytes.

🔴 **AND THE MATRIX PATH'S TWO OUTPUT MATRICES ARE A COPY, WHICH THE CODE SAYS
THE OPPOSITE OF TWICE.** `af3TriangleWeights` transposes `output_projection`
into the AF2 convention and `transposeZG` transposes it back for the matrix
kernel; composed they are the identity.

Gated in `check-bundle-device-decode.js` on all three real bundles, each arm
with the control that says it is testing anything:

    transpose      3981312 elements  0 differing  3979346 positions separate it from doing nothing
    interleave-2   3981312           0            3979340
    f32-stride4     294912           0 differing  0 outside its lane

🔴 **AND THE FIRST VERSION OF THAT CONTROL WAS COUNTING COINCIDENCES.** It asked
how many elements a reshape leaves where they were, and on OpenDDE's pair
transition that is 99.7% - not because the permutation is near-identity but
because int5 gives a group 32 distinct values, so a moved element usually lands
on a position holding its own bits. What the control has to measure is how many
positions would separate a correct shader from one that ignored the mapping
entirely, and 7676 of 2,359,296 is plenty.

🔴 **AND `check-af3-block-any.js --resident` IS THE GATE FOR ALL OF IT**,
because residency is a different WEIGHT PATH and not a cache. Host-packed
against device-packed, to every digit:

    OpenDDE, matrix path   0.018417181287129433 both ways
    OpenDDE, vector path   0.11147478014586483  both ways
    AF3                    0.0308058352612606   both ways

## A measurement re-asked and declined: f16 pair weights on AF3

The rule that keeps AF3's pair track in f32 was written as "247 MiB to buy
nothing" - f16 halves the peak only where the track is wide, and AF3's peak is
the diffusion transformer regardless. What f16 buys NOW is the device decode
path, which is a different currency, so it was re-measured: AF3's first fold is
5.6 s at f32 and 5.3 at f16, and the second is 3.1 either way.

Declined. Three hundred milliseconds of a first fold is not enough to loosen the
numerics of the one model in this repository that has an oracle, and the old
note already measured the fold shifting (mean pLDDT 72.19283791929007 ->
72.17922675038298). Written down so the next person does not re-derive it.

## Where the five models stand, after all of this

| | first fold | second |
|---|---:|---:|
| AF2 monomer, 59 res | 1.45 s | 0.20 s |
| AF2 multimer, 59 res | 1.45 s | 0.21 s |
| ESMFold2, 40 res | 3.32 s | 0.86 s |
| AF3, 68 tokens, 200 steps | 5.5 s | 3.1 s |
| OpenDDE, 6MRR, 200 steps | 7.61 s | 4.46 s |

OpenDDE started this session at **20.8 s**.

What is left is the sampler, and it is compute: at 130 structural tokens a
denoiser call is 22 ms of wall against 17.8 of labelled GPU, and the token
transformer is 13.25 of that -

    attend 2.73   qkvg 1.99   ffw-wide 1.89   ffw-out 1.44   attention-output 1.07
    zero-gates 0.99   ffw-wide-reduce 0.65   qkvg-reduce 0.64   adaln 0.48
    ffw-adaln 0.48   ffw-out-reduce 0.47   attention-output-reduce 0.42

- four dense projections and their four K-split reductions, on the vector units.

## Three things that did not work, measured

Written down because each looked obviously right and each cost an afternoon.

**The matrix units on the diffusion transformer.** Covered above: a tie at
`qkvg`'s shape, and the 1.46x at `ffw-out`'s is the workgroup count rather than
the units. The shipped kernel is already 3.6x the best generic GEMM in the tree
at the same M, K and N.

**Moving ESMFold2's weight narrowing before the trunk.** `prepare` is 966 ms on
a first fold and 55 on a second, the compile is only 85 of that, and the rest is
narrowing 459 MiB of token blocks to f16. That depends on nothing but the
weights, so it can run while the trunk does - the same trick the AF3 head's
`warm` uses for its pipelines. Built it: `#residentBlocks` extracted, yielding a
macrotask between blocks so the trunk's continuations get through. Measured at
300 residues:

    no weight warm   5.061 s, 5.098   language model 1018 ms, cond 1146
    weight warm      5.038,  5.042    language model 1784,    cond  452

**The work moved and nothing else did.** The host is not idle during ESMFold2's
trunk: the tower's block loop runs on it, and the language-model stage simply
absorbed the 700 ms. Reverted; the pipeline warm, which IS off the main thread,
is kept and is worth 966 -> 881.

**Re-sweeping the denoiser's knobs.** `diffusionAttendSubgroups`,
`diffusionNormSubgroups` and the token tile are all null in the Ampere prior and
all three stay null: at 130 tokens the subgroup arms are 16 ms either way, and
the tile medians 33 ms at 1, 2, 4 and the default over three interleaved rounds.
`bench-af2-warm.js` says the same for AF2's three ungated matrix knobs -
`opmMatrixOutput`, `stagedMatrixDirectWeights`, `stagedMatrixResult` measure
3.456, 3.446 and 3.429 against the default's 3.429. And `probe-tuning.js` still
`agrees` with the shipped table.

🔴 **ONE THING THE TILE SWEEP DID FIND.** AF2's matrix flash attention at
`attentionMatrixTile=4x64` folds a DIFFERENT structure - checksum -2857868
against 4x32's -2940090 - deterministically, and 14% slower. It is not a bug:
`check-attention-variants.js` passes at 4x16, 4x32, 2x32, 6x32, 8x32 and 4x64,
so the kernel computes the same thing at every tile. A 64-key tile reassociates
the online softmax, and 48 blocks times three recycles turn that into a
different structure. Any future tile sweep will see it; it is reassociation, not
a tile the dispatch does not match.

## The pair stops round-tripping through the host

`fold-opendde.js --length=200 --buffers`, on a 384-structural-token fold, before
any of this:

    3006 ms  29.2%  x79    995.5 MiB  mapAsync
      389 ms         x3    490.6 MiB  mapAsync af3-block.readback-pair
      343 ms         x1    216.0 MiB  mapAsync expand.read-pair

**mapAsync runs at about 1.26 GB/s here**, and every one of those tensors went
straight back to the device. The structural path is four GPU stages - expander,
refiner, diffusion conditioning, confidence head - handing a `tokens^2 x 384`
tensor to each other through a host Float32Array.

`Af3StructuralExpanderGpu.run` and `Af3PairformerStackGpu.run` take `keepPair`
and hand back the allocation; the refiner takes the expander's buffer as its
`pairBuffer` and updates it IN PLACE, because a pair track is a residual chain -
so one buffer is the pair from the expansion to the sampler, and the expander is
what owns it. The conditioning takes `trunkPairBuffer`; the confidence head
takes `pairBuffer` for its own pair init and then keeps its stack's output there
too, since PAE and PDE are kernels now.

| 200 residues, 384 structural tokens | warm | first |
|---|---:|---:|
| before | 7797 ms | 10.4 s |
| the refiner's pair kept | 7217 | 9.54 |
| ...and the expander's | 6719 | 9.25 |
| ...and the confidence head's | **5989** | **8.48** |

    structural-expand   398 ms -> 116      structural-refine 1167 -> 439
    structural-conditioning 305 -> 118     sample-done 1367 -> 746

and the profile afterwards: mapAsync 995.5 MiB -> 347.5, of which 5.3 MiB is the
sampler's fifty `dec.rb` readbacks and the rest is the trunk's own pair, which
still goes to the host because the recycle loop and the returned trunk read it.

🔴 **IT COSTS 213 MiB OF PEAK**, and the release is where it is for that reason:
the pair is held across the sampler because the confidence head reads it
afterwards. 2968.5 -> 3181.1 MiB at 200 residues. On a device with a ceiling
that is the wrong side of the trade, and measuring it before taking this
pattern further is the next person's job.

🔴 **AND A STACK HANDED A BUFFER DOES NOT OWN IT.** `pairAllocation` comes back
only when the stack allocated the pair itself. Given `options.pairBuffer` it
updates the caller's in place, and handing back something with no `release`
would have been a footgun with a plausible shape.

| AF2, 400 res x 512 rows x 1024 extra | 4.15 s | 3.43 s |

| OpenDDE 6MRR, 68 res / 130 structural tokens, 200 steps | 6.68 s | 4.14 s |
| OpenDDE, 200 res / 384 structural tokens, 50 steps | 8.48 s | 5.99 s |

And in the shipped PAGE, which is the other thing that has to keep working -
`tools/fold-in-page.py`, a fresh profile with an empty cache each time, so the
weight download is in these:

    monomer   Done in 2.5 s   pLDDT 61.9   pTM 0.320
    multimer  Done in 2.4 s   pLDDT 65.9   pTM 0.407
    af3       58 residues, 16 steps, in 3 s, pLDDT 67.9
    ef2-fast-600m  58 res, 11 steps, 3.9 s, certainty 0.59
    opendde   58 residues, 4 steps, in 8 s (5 s with the shard cache warm), pLDDT 83.6

OpenDDE's 6MRR fold started this session at **20.8 s**; ESMFold2's first fold at
**8.0 s**. What did NOT move is AF2 at 400 residues - 3.43 s, unchanged, because
its stack's wall time equals its labelled GPU time to three digits and every
knob in `probe-tuning.js` still agrees with the shipped table.

Where the remaining time is, and it is not the same place it was:

- **AF3 and OpenDDE warm folds are the sampler**, 70-85% of them, and a sampler
  step is 93% GPU (`probe-sampler-overhead.js`). The token transformer is 74% of
  a denoiser call and its own kernels are 3.6x the best generic GEMM in the tree
  at the same shapes, so this is a floor rather than an oversight.
- **First folds are pipeline compilation**, now that the packing has gone: a
  cold denoiser call is 1360 ms against 16 warm, of which 946 is the transformer
  and is already warmed during the trunk. The other 410 - the conditioning, the
  atom encoder and the atom decoder - is not, and is the next thing.
- **AF2 is kernels**, and its block at 400 residues is `msa-row-attention` 13.48
  ms, `msa-column-attention` 11.16, `msa-transition` 9.33 over 75 dispatches,
  `opm` 7.75, the triangle multiplications 6.42 and the two triangle attentions
  9.53.

## Is the page on the GPU? Yes, and here is the arithmetic

Measured with `tools/fold-in-page.py`, which drives the real page in a real
Chrome, headful under `DISPLAY=:99`. The page's own timer, on a 58-residue
chain:

| | fresh profile (empty shard cache) | second run, cache warm |
|---|---:|---:|
| AF2 monomer | 2.5 s | - |
| AF2 multimer | 2.4 s | - |
| AlphaFold 3, 16 steps | 3 s | 3 s |
| EF2-fast-600m, 11 steps | 3.9 s | - |
| OpenDDE, 4 steps | 8 s | **5 s** |

Those are the same order as the command-line folds - AF2 1.45 s, AF3 5.2 s at
200 steps, ESMFold2 3.3 s - so the page is getting the same adapter the tools
do. A SwiftShader fallback is not a 20% difference, it is two orders of
magnitude, and this box's whole Xvfb-and-headful recipe exists because
`--headless=new` silently hands you one.

**And the weights ARE re-downloaded on every run of that harness**, because it
launches a fresh Chrome profile and the shard cache is a Cache API store that
dies with it. From a local server that is 0 s on AF3's 265 MiB (3 s either way,
at this timer's resolution) and **3 s of OpenDDE's 8** on its 472. Over the
internet it is the whole first-visit cost, which is what the Cache API is for -
see docs/HOSTING.md.

🔴 **AND A TRUNCATED SHARD WAS BEING CACHED.** `#downloadFile` stores a clone of
the response before reading the body, which is right, and then a short stream
threw - so the clone stayed, and every reload read the same short bytes back out
of the cache. It retries three times now, deleting the cache entry between
attempts, and the error names both halves: `11704548 bytes arrived where the
manifest says 40891008`.

🔴 **AND THAT MESSAGE IS WHAT FOUND THE REAL FAULT, WHICH WAS NOT A DOWNLOAD.**
11704548 is exactly the size of the file on disk. The bundle in
`model-opendde-int5/` was a **32-shard** export; the registry pins a
**12-shard** one. The CLI tools read the `manifest.json` sitting next to the
shards and were perfectly happy - every `fold-opendde.js` gate passed, RMSD
1.655 - while the page reads the manifest baked into
`src/reference/manifests/opendde.js` and could not load a byte of it. Same
weights either way: with the pinned bundle in place the CLI gives pLDDT
92.05079322575692 and RMSD 1.655, the same digits, and the page folds in 8 s.

**Check a bundle against the MODULE, not against the JSON beside it.**

## AF2's weights decode on the device: 2.37 s -> 1.79, and the page 2.5 -> 2.0

AF2 was the one model this session had not touched, and the reason was that its
kernels are at their floor - `bench-af2-warm.js` at 400 residues is 3.43 s and
the stack's wall equals its labelled GPU time to three digits. Its WEIGHTS were
not.

🔴 **THE FOLD'S CLOCK STARTS AFTER THE WEIGHTS ARE LOADED, AND A USER'S DOES
NOT.** `weightLoadMs` was already in `fold-af2.js`'s output and nothing had
added it to anything: 971 ms on top of a 1400 ms first fold. Split, it is ~275
ms of download and **445 ms of host dequantisation** - AF2's 98 MiB bundle is
int8, 283 tensors of 337, 93.1 M elements. Then another **370 ms** inside the
fold concatenating those arrays into the buffers the kernels bind.

That loop cannot be written faster. Benched at 8 Mi elements: the shipped form
254 Melem/s, unrolled eight ways 235, with a constant inner bound 160, and
`out[i] = codes[i]` with no arithmetic at all **263**. So it had to stop running.

|  | load | first fold | total | second |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| monomer before | 971 ms | 1400 | 2371 | 210 |
| monomer after | **492** | **1296** | **1788** | 215 |
| multimer before | 968 | 1450 | 2418 | 219 |
| multimer after | **505** | **1230** | **1735** | 217 |
| in the page, monomer | 2.5 s | | **2.0 s** | |
| in the page, multimer | 2.4 s | | **1.9 s** | |

pLDDT 57.32 and 54.234, `caca` 3.854 and 3.756, checksum -2940090 - the same
fold at every point, and all nine AF2 checkers unchanged.

Four pieces:

- **int8 in the decoder.** Two differences from int5 and not one: two's
  complement codes, and a symmetric codec - a scale per group and no zero point.
  `codecOf` carries `signed`, the shader sign-extends and drops the zero read,
  and the planner stops requiring a `zeroOffset` while still BINDING a zeros
  buffer, because the bind group layout is one layout for every codec.
- **`#gather` in the fixture**, which is `#parameter` for a list of properties:
  with a store that can hand over bytes it returns getters and a `SOURCES` map.
  A stacked tensor's per-block slice becomes `first`/`count` in the source,
  which is what `planBlockUpload` already took.
- **The packers export their order**, and the host packer maps over the same
  list - a list written twice is a list that ends up written differently.
- **`uploadResidentPacked`**, async because filling a buffer from codes is a
  compute pass.

Three things this cost, each of which was invisible:

🔴 **A PAIR-BIAS DESCRIPTOR BUILT FROM VALUES IS A DECODE.** Ten literals across
the two block files wrote `layerNormScale: row.pairLayerNormScale` into a fresh
object, which READS the getter. `Object.create(row.pairBias)` keeps the getters
and the `SOURCES` symbol reachable.

🔴 **AND THE FALLBACK HID IT.** The first version returned `undefined` when it
could not take a buffer and the caller packed on the host - correct, silent, and
300 ms slower, with no error and no wrong number. `DeviceWeightRefusal` names
the tensor and the reason and is thrown; `allowHostWeightPacking` is what a
bundle that genuinely cannot be decoded sets, and it is null by default. Four
refusals fell out in a row, each naming its own descriptor, and each was a
literal that copied instead of deriving.

🔴 **AND GATING ON "NO BUDGET" GAVE THE WHOLE THING TO THE TOOLS AND NONE OF IT
TO THE PAGE.** The condition was copied from `uploadResident` beside it, which
declines under a ceiling because the POOLED allocator evicts to make room and
can evict a buffer an in-flight submit still names. This path allocates through
`noteAllocation`, which raises before `createBuffer` and evicts nothing - so it
runs under a budget, with a quarter of the ceiling as the line, and
`packedBytesOf` answers that before the first allocation. Without the line,
`--budget=200` reproduced the eviction in one run.

**And `--budget=200` is 151 ms slower than it was**, 2515 against 2364. Below
the line the device path is declined and the lazy fixture decodes inside the
fold rather than at load - the same work in a worse place. It buys 563 ms
everywhere above the line.
